# FlagQuantum Capabilities

Choose a supported workflow by user goal, runtime, hardware, and evidence level.
This catalog is generated from the machine-validated
[`capability-maturity.toml`](../../capability-maturity.toml) source of truth.

> Maturity applies only to the scope stated in each row. A local, replicated,
> sliced, or planned execution path is not distributed scalability evidence.

## How to read maturity

| Level | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| **Release certified** | Release-gated with audited, reproducible evidence and no unresolved release blocker. |
| **Production supported** | Supported path with compatibility, operational guidance, and target-hardware evidence. |
| **Development evidence** | Executable and tested development result; not a production or general scalability claim. |
| **Experimental** | Research surface without compatibility or production guarantees. |

## Build and compile

### Unified circuit API and FlagQuantum IR

Build, validate, serialize, compile, and inspect quantum circuits through the stable FlagQuantum interface.

- **Maturity:** Release certified
- **Public API:** `fq.Circuit`, `fq.CircuitIR`, `flagquantum.compiler.compile`
- **Runtime modes:** `not_applicable`
- **Hardware:** `cpu`
- **Gradient support:** `not_applicable`
- **Distribution semantics:** `not_applicable`
- **Start:** [quick example](../../examples/quick_start.py)
- **Documentation:** [guide](../../docs/reference/API.md)
- **Known boundary:** IR v1; incompatible schema changes require an explicit migration.

### Program optimization and target-aware compilation

Transform one FlagQuantum IR program without executing it: canonical optimization, logical layer scheduling, and lowering onto an explicit coupling topology.

- **Maturity:** Production supported
- **Public API:** `flagquantum.compiler.optimize`, `flagquantum.compiler.compile`, `flagquantum.compiler.route_to_topology`, `flagquantum.compiler.schedule_layers`, `flagquantum.compiler.CouplingMap`, `flagquantum.compiler.LinkErrorRates`, `flagquantum.compiler.plan_trivial_layout`, `flagquantum.compiler.plan_dense_layout`, `flagquantum.compiler.PassManager`, `flagquantum.compiler.PassRegistry`, `flagquantum.compiler.default_pass_registry`, `flagquantum.compiler.OPTIMIZATION_PIPELINE`
- **Runtime modes:** `not_applicable`
- **Hardware:** `cpu`
- **Gradient support:** `not_applicable`
- **Distribution semantics:** `not_applicable`
- **Start:** [quick example](../../examples/compiler_optimize.py)
- **Documentation:** [guide](../../flagquantum/compiler/README.md)
- **Known boundary:** In-process transformation of one FlagQuantum IR v1 program on CPU; the compiler never executes a program or certifies hardware behavior. optimize() removes identity gates, merges self-inverse runs, and merges adjacent rotations to a fixed point. compile() runs that same fixed point, optionally routes onto one explicit CouplingMap, optimizes again, and records post-routing optimization. Routing offers four caller-selected strategies: restore_after_each_gate, persistent_layout, sabre, and sabre_layout. All four restore the identity logical order at the program output, so a caller cannot ask for an unrestored final permutation, and the cost-estimate automatic selection covers only restore_after_each_gate and persistent_layout because the two SABRE strategies plan SWAPs instead of estimating them; the topology is always caller-supplied and never discovered. A device may also state what each coupling costs: LinkErrorRates pairs a CouplingMap with one measured two-qubit error probability per link, and a route then costs the expected number of fault events its links carry rather than the number of hops, so a longer route over clean links can be chosen over a shorter one over dirty links. Such a device narrows the automatic selection to the two strategies that read the rate and refuses the two SABRE strategies by name, because plan_sabre_swaps takes no per-link weight, so it would route by hop count while reporting a price the calibration had no part in. RoutingCostEstimate.expected_link_faults reports the total the plan pays and is None on a device that states only its couplings, so a caller who passes a CouplingMap gets the plan it got before. Initial placement is planned separately: plan_trivial_layout places logical qubit i on physical qubit i, and plan_dense_layout places the program on the most densely connected window of device qubits a breadth-first walk from every physical qubit finds, which is the only placement that can help on a device with unequal connectivity and the only one of the two that can reach a program the lowest qubits cannot hold at all. That walk is a heuristic and not an exact optimum: over 55 device and program-width combinations it matched an exhaustive search of every connected window 45 times and returned a window with one fewer internal connection the other 10 times. Both return a placement as a tuple of physical qubits, one per logical qubit, which is what route_to_directed_topology takes as initial_layout; final_layout reports the assignment a routed program ended on as a Layout, and a route that allocates workspace on a device wider than the program reports the device width there with its idle slots as None in physical_to_logical. Both placements collapse to the identity on a device no wider than the program. Neither reaches route_to_topology, which refuses an initial_layout because a plain CouplingMap routes only over qubits the program owns; a non-identity placement requires a DirectedCouplingMap, whose physical workspace owns the idle slots and is cleaned by the inverse routing SWAPs, and it is passed to route_to_directed_topology or to legalize_circuit_topology. remove_layout_restore drops the appended logical-restore phase from a program that was already routed, halving the SWAP count over a batch of random programs for persistent_layout because that strategy's restore is a full reverse replay of its forward SWAPs and leaving restore_after_each_gate unchanged because its restores are interleaved with the program, and its result is a program for a local simulator or a provider that accepts per-physical-qubit results rather than deployable routing evidence: the deployment routing contract rejects it and the deployment entry points treat it as unrouted and route it again. Window selection is greedy over device connectivity and never estimates routing cost, so it can cost more SWAPs than the trivial placement on a program whose interactions prefer a sparser window, and it is not noise- or calibration-aware. Scheduling constructs deterministic logical ASAP layers with explicit qubit and classical-data dependencies; it is not target timing or pulse scheduling. There is no directed acyclic graph intermediate over the IR, and a pass is a Python function over a whole program rather than a rewrite rule with a pattern and a replacement. The pipeline itself is a named sequence of registered passes, each declaring the analyses it reads and preserves, so a caller can admit a pass of its own by name through optimize(..., passes=...) or compile(..., passes=...), or register one with declarations on default_pass_registry() and run it over OPTIMIZATION_PIPELINE through PassManager; a built-in name cannot be shadowed, an admission next to optimize=False is refused rather than ignored, and the admitted names are recorded on the program as an admitted_passes entry in its metadata while the optimization entry keeps its level. An installed external compiler is still selected by name through the extension SDK rather than composed as a pass. Pulse-level and parameter-aware compilation, dynamic-circuit control flow, and noise-aware optimization beyond lower_noise_model are unsupported. Calibration enters routing as one number per link and nowhere else: no readout fidelity, gate duration, or two-qubit fidelity stated apart from its error probability is priced, correlated and gate-dependent error is not expressible, and no route is a bridge gate, so every route is still a chain of hops between declared pairs. A SABRE search weighted by a calibration is absent and is refused rather than approximated. The structured hybrid program slice under flagquantum/compiler/_hybrid is private and absent from public exports. Compilation is single-process; no distributed or multi-device compilation path exists.

### Target-basis gate and state-preparation synthesis

Spell gates and states a target cannot express in the gates that target publishes, and report a basis that cannot carry them instead of approximating it.

- **Maturity:** Development evidence
- **Public API:** `flagquantum.compiler.native_gate_legalization.legalize_native_gates`, `flagquantum.compiler.one_qubit_synthesis.synthesize_one_qubit`, `flagquantum.compiler.two_qubit_synthesis.synthesize_two_qubit`, `flagquantum.compiler.state_preparation_synthesis.synthesize_state_preparation`, `flagquantum.compiler.basis_translation.translate`
- **Runtime modes:** `not_applicable`
- **Hardware:** `cpu`
- **Gradient support:** `not_applicable`
- **Distribution semantics:** `not_applicable`
- **Start:** [quick example](../../examples/compiler_synthesis.py)
- **Documentation:** [guide](../../flagquantum/compiler/README.md)
- **Known boundary:** In-process target-basis rewriting of one FlagQuantum IR v1 program on CPU; it never executes a program, chooses a target, or certifies hardware behavior, and no performance, fidelity, or hardware claim is made. The synthesis entry points are deliberately not stable flagquantum.compiler exports: synthesize_one_qubit, synthesize_two_qubit and synthesize_state_preparation are reached by module path, and only legalize_native_gates applies them as a whole-program pass. A synthesized rewrite equals its source only up to one global phase, which FlagQuantum IR has no field to record, so every entry point refuses rather than approximating. The refusals are per entry point rather than uniform: synthesize_one_qubit declines z_rotation='u3' and pulse_opcode='ry' while it does accept z_rotation='phase' for a single gate, and the state-preparation ladder declines 'phase' and 'u1' at the same keyword because a ladder needs an entrywise-exact rz. synthesize_two_qubit declines any entangler outside the supercontrolled table, 'ecr' among them, and it is handed a matrix rather than an instruction because the matrix of a named two-qubit gate belongs to flagquantum.simulation, which this layer must not import; it holds only the three parameter-free entangler matrices and the closed form of the rotation family, so a named two-qubit opcode reaches it only after the equivalence table has rewritten it or has reported the gate the target is missing. That table declares eighteen identities from named opcodes to named opcodes and composes them recursively, because a named gate carries no matrix on this layer; an entry that is not itself exact therefore does not stay exact under further expansion, and cphase drops a global phase. For ccx the table's T-form was measured against Qiskit's MCXGrayCode statement and kept: the Gray-code form declares seven leaves against fifteen but costs eight two-qubit gates on the interaction basis against six, and its further expansion runs through cphase, the one table entry that is not exact. Multi-controlled decomposition above three controls is unsupported: FlagQuantum IR declares no arity above three, Qiskit builds MCXGrayCode from an ancilla chain only from five controls, and the V-chain and recursive forms that do cover four controls and above use fewer entanglers than Gray code but need caller-supplied ancillas the IR does not model. The one-qubit angle form and the two-qubit block form are exact only to the working precision of their own arithmetic, and a rewrite whose leaves leave the native set is reported as the opcode the target is missing rather than as the shortest near miss. Synthesis is single-process CPU work with no distributed, multi-device, pulse-level, calibration-aware, or noise-aware path.

### Constrained coherent integer synthesis

Construct a checked reversible arithmetic circuit from bounded Python unsigned arithmetic and one-bit ==/< predicates composed with and/or/not, then execute and read named registers through the existing runtime.

- **Maturity:** Development evidence
- **Public API:** `flagquantum.synth.synthesize`, `flagquantum.synth.Program.probabilities`, `fq.run`
- **Runtime modes:** `statevector`
- **Hardware:** `cpu`
- **Gradient support:** `bound_scalar_preparation_pytorch_autograd`
- **Distribution semantics:** `single_device_fast_path`
- **Start:** [quick example](../../examples/algorithms/synthesis_trial.py)
- **Documentation:** [guide](../../flagquantum/synth/README.md)
- **Known boundary:** Unreleased unsigned-integer CPU profile with bounded Python syntax including single ==/< comparisons and one-bit and/or/not expressions, known input preparations and finite checked candidates; no arbitrary Python or Boolean control flow, general Qmod, Program/.fq serialization, noise, measurements, directed or allocated-layout admission, or optimality guarantee. A Program executes its checked plan and refuses execution overrides; changed constraints require resynthesis. Two independent developer trials and release review remain pending.

### Coherent predicate phase synthesis

Construct an exact marked sign from a verified one-bit predicate through the shared Compiler schedules and existing checked Program.

- **Maturity:** Development evidence
- **Public API:** `flagquantum.synth.synthesize`, `flagquantum.synth.Program.probabilities`, `fq.run`
- **Runtime modes:** `statevector`
- **Hardware:** `cpu`
- **Gradient support:** `not_applicable`
- **Distribution semantics:** `single_device_fast_path`
- **Start:** [quick example](../../examples/synthesis_phase_oracle.py)
- **Documentation:** [guide](../../docs/roadmap/SYNTH_PUBLIC_PHASE_DELIVERY.md)
- **Known boundary:** Unreleased Python one-bit predicates admit output_mode=phase through the existing public Program/run/read flow on native logical CPU only, with no result flag. Wider returns, Qmod phase and explicit basis/topology are refused before source work where knowable. Exact phase and clean workspace are verified; controlled-marker conjugation remains private evidence only. Conservative line polynomial probes pass, but equality line is explicitly refused by the retained routed-SWAP witness guard; no general line, projective conversion or generic controlled-Circuit legalization claim. Zero workspace is a declared precondition, not an external-state proof. No Qmod phase, arbitrary angles, within-apply DSL, all-corpus or release admission. Independent trials remain 0/2.

### Circuit drawing

Render one circuit as terminal text or a Matplotlib figure through one shared layout, without executing it.

- **Maturity:** Experimental
- **Public API:** `flagquantum.drawer.draw`, `flagquantum.drawer.draw_text`, `flagquantum.drawer.use_style`, `flagquantum.drawer.available_styles`
- **Runtime modes:** `not_applicable`
- **Hardware:** `cpu`
- **Gradient support:** `not_applicable`
- **Distribution semantics:** `not_applicable`
- **Start:** [quick-start guide](../../flagquantum/drawer/README.md)
- **Documentation:** [guide](../../flagquantum/drawer/README.md)
- **Known boundary:** Presentation only. The drawer owns layout, labels, gate symbols, and styles; it does not execute, compile, validate, or route a circuit, and it changes no numerical result. The text renderer depends only on the standard library and Core, while Matplotlib is optional and is imported only by the figure renderer, so draw_mpl is absent from flagquantum.drawer when Matplotlib is not installed. The rendered text layout is not a frozen contract: no compatibility guarantee is made for exact spacing, gate symbols, column placement, or option names, and the only tests assert renderer self-consistency rather than a golden diagram. Rendering is single-process CPU work with no streaming or interactive backend. Structured hybrid programs, pulse-level schedules, and target timing diagrams are not drawable. The package README is the only user-facing documentation.

### Construction-time program composition

Build a larger program out of smaller ones in place: place one program on chosen qubits, invert a program, apply a program a whole number of times, and run a program only when added control qubits are all set, without introducing a second IR.

- **Maturity:** Development evidence
- **Public API:** `flagquantum.Circuit.compose`, `flagquantum.Circuit.adjoint`, `flagquantum.Circuit.power`, `flagquantum.Circuit.control`
- **Runtime modes:** `not_applicable`
- **Hardware:** `cpu`
- **Gradient support:** `not_applicable`
- **Distribution semantics:** `not_applicable`
- **Start:** [quick-start guide](../../docs/reference/API.md)
- **Documentation:** [guide](../../docs/reference/API.md)
- **Known boundary:** All four methods are Stable Core additions to the fq.Circuit type, approved by docs/api-changes/FQ-CIRCUIT-COMPOSITION-20261002.md, docs/api-changes/FQ-CIRCUIT-ADJOINT-20261003.md, docs/api-changes/FQ-CIRCUIT-POWER-20261021.md, docs/api-changes/FQ-CIRCUIT-CONTROL-20261022.md and, for the carried-operand route of Circuit.control, docs/api-changes/FQ-CIRCUIT-CONTROL-OPERAND-20261022.md, and the four-member family is complete: every operation it names has an approved proposal. A noise channel, a dynamic operation, a classically conditioned instruction, a matrix without a conjugate transpose and an unknown opcode without a matrix are each refused with CapabilityError rather than copied forward. Composition, inversion, control and repetition are rewrites of qubit labels and not a second program description: none of the four changes IR_VERSION, each emits instructions the IR already describes, and none adds a root export, so no existing program changes meaning. Circuit.power returns a new circuit that applies the receiver's program an integer number of times, and it is exact for every instruction: the whole program is appended in order, so a gate with no angle, a custom operation carrying a matrix and a noise channel all work, and a negative exponent is adjoint().power(-k) so that the inverse rule keeps one implementation. One rewrite shortens the common case -- a receiver that is exactly one instruction declaring exactly one parameter has that parameter multiplied by the exponent instead of the gate being repeated -- and the rule belongs to the gate, declared as a power_rule on every opcode and measured through the dense operator by tools/check_circuit_composition_contract.py: 12 of the 35 registered opcodes take the closed form and the other 23 repeat, with u2 and u3 repeating because scaling all of their angles is a different operator. power(1) copies rather than multiplying by one, so a symbolic parameter or a trainable angle stays the same object, and power(0) is the empty program of the same shape, which keeps p.power(a).power(b) equal to p.power(a * b) at a = 0. A fractional exponent is an owned gap: the IR has no instruction form for a matrix root, so power(0.5) raises TypeError rather than approximating, while PennyLane 0.45.1 constructs and runs qml.Hadamard(0) ** 0.5; closing it needs a new Instruction form and is therefore an IR change rather than a composition change. Requests that would emit more than 4096 instructions are refused with ValueError naming the count. Circuit.control returns a new circuit in which every gate runs only when the control qubits it added are all set. The control qubits are added rather than borrowed, so each label must lie outside the receiver's own range and the result's width covers them, and an input state set on the receiver is carried into the wider register with every added qubit in |0>. The controlled form belongs to the opcode and is declared once in flagquantum/core/operator_schema.py next to the inversion and shift rules: 31 of the 35 registered opcodes declare one, the four that do not are the noise channels, and a channel, a dynamic operation and a classically conditioned instruction are refused with CapabilityError rather than approximated, while an instruction that carries its own matrix is wrapped instead: no opcode declares the controlled form of a caller's matrix, so the operand itself is placed in the block diagonal diag(I, U), which is the identity on every branch a clear control selects and the operand on the branch where all of them are set, recorded as one instruction spanning the controls and the operand's own wires. The price of that route is the operand's size rather than the ladder's depth, and a matrix whose shape does not say how many entries one qubit spans is refused rather than guessed at. A gate the registry already has in controlled form is used directly, so x under one control is cx and swap under one control is cswap, and every wider control is emitted as an ancilla-free phase ladder of registered gates, which keeps a symbolic angle symbolic and the result inside the autograd graph. The price is depth and it is published: a w-qubit block under k controls needs a ladder of level k + w - 1, which emits on the order of 3 ** level instructions, MAX_LADDER_LEVEL is 10, and a request past the ceiling is refused by name. The surface is single-process CPU work on one program with no execution, compilation, routing, noise handling, or scalability claim, and it is not differentiable by itself because composition records instructions rather than evaluating them. contracts/circuit-composition-contract.toml is the authoritative list of these guarantees and of every refusal, and tests/unit/test_circuit_composition_contract.py reads it back against the implementation."

### OpenQASM 2 and OpenQASM 3 interchange

Write one FlagQuantum program as OpenQASM text and read that text back as the same program, with every other text refused by name.

- **Maturity:** Production supported
- **Public API:** `fq.from_openqasm`, `flagquantum.compiler.openqasm.emit_openqasm`
- **Runtime modes:** `not_applicable`
- **Hardware:** `cpu`
- **Gradient support:** `bound_parameters_only`
- **Distribution semantics:** `not_applicable`
- **Start:** [quick-start guide](../../flagquantum/compiler/README.md)
- **Documentation:** [guide](../../flagquantum/compiler/README.md)
- **Known boundary:** Text interchange only; the importer never executes a program and does not make FlagQuantum an OpenQASM front end. Import accepts exactly the canonical subset the emitter writes and verifies that claim by re-emitting the parsed program and requiring statement equivalence, so text that denotes something the emitter would write differently is refused rather than interpreted approximately, and arbitrary third-party OpenQASM is out of scope. Both 2.0 and 3.0 are read; the version and its matching include statement are mandatory, so a program declaring any other version is refused as unsupported_version. One qreg or qubit register and one creg or bit register may be declared, and a program declaring more is malformed_statement. Because OpenQASM 3 spells the three-angle gate as U and has no two-angle gate, U is read as u3 unconditionally: u2(phi, lbd) and U(pi/2, phi, lbd) denote the same unitary and the emitter writes the same text for both. The inverse of sx is the one power accepted, spelled pow(-1) @ sx q[i]; every other power is unknown_gate. Parameters must be bound numbers, so rx(theta) q[0]; is unbound_parameter. barrier and reset are not opcodes and are refused. The measurement block must be terminal, must define every classical bit exactly once, each bit must read a distinct qubit, and a classical register wider than the quantum register or a partly defined measurement block is incomplete_measurement. Comments, blank lines, and spacing carry no meaning; any other textual difference from the canonical emission is not_canonical_text. Eleven refusal reasons form a closed vocabulary, each carried on OpenQASMImportError.issue_code, which is the first issue_code in the package. Import returns an OpenQASMImport rather than a Circuit because a Circuit cannot carry a measurement, and IR_VERSION stays 1.0 because import happens at construction time.

### QIR base-profile emission

Emit canonical FlagQuantum IR as QIR base-profile LLVM IR text: the entry point, the QIS calls for the lowered program, and the runtime calls that record the output.

- **Maturity:** Development evidence
- **Public API:** `flagquantum.compiler.qir`, `flagquantum.compiler.qir.emit_qir`, `flagquantum.compiler.qir.ENTRY_POINT_NAME`, `flagquantum.compiler.qir.OUTPUT_LABELING_SCHEMA`, `flagquantum.compiler.translate`
- **Runtime modes:** `not_applicable`
- **Hardware:** `cpu`
- **Gradient support:** `not_applicable`
- **Distribution semantics:** `not_applicable`
- **Start:** [quick-start guide](../../flagquantum/compiler/README.md)
- **Documentation:** [guide](../../flagquantum/compiler/README.md)
- **Known boundary:** Text emission of one program on CPU, and nothing more: no LLVM compilation to an object file or an executable, no linking, no execution, and no external QIR validator was run, so conformance is the repository's own semantic check plus a host C parser accepting the text as LLVM IR syntax. The base profile is the whole of it. Profile-QIR and the adaptive profile are absent, and because both dynamic-management module flags are false, so are __quantum__rt__qubit_allocate_array and __quantum__rt__qubit_release_array and every read_result call; a program therefore cannot allocate a qubit at runtime through this emitter, which is the same gap the runtime qubit allocation row records on the IR side. Pulse-level generation and runtime library extension calls are absent. Only the constant-index pointer kind is emitted: a qubit or result operand is ptr null for index 0 and ptr inttoptr (i64 k to ptr) otherwise. An opcode with no QIS instruction is lowered to a native sequence whose unitary equals the requested gate up to an unconditional global phase, which the base profile cannot express and recorded measurement output cannot observe, so the emitted program is equivalent for every measurable outcome and conformance is checked with a global-phase-invariant overlap rather than elementwise; a gate whose semantics the base profile cannot carry at all is refused by index and opcode rather than approximated. A noise channel, a classical condition, a dynamic instruction, a reset, an arbitrary matrix gate, and an observable request are among what it refuses.

### Static resource estimation over a program's instruction sequence

Report the operations, the schedule depth, the per-wire depth, the T family, and the widest operation of a static program by running the compiler's own scheduler over its instruction sequence.

- **Maturity:** Development evidence
- **Public API:** `flagquantum.compiler.resource_estimation`, `flagquantum.compiler.resource_estimation.estimate_resources`, `flagquantum.compiler.resource_estimation.ResourceEstimate`, `flagquantum.compiler.resource_estimation.ESTIMATE_BASIS`
- **Runtime modes:** `not_applicable`
- **Hardware:** `cpu`
- **Gradient support:** `not_applicable`
- **Distribution semantics:** `not_applicable`
- **Start:** [quick-start guide](../../flagquantum/compiler/README.md)
- **Documentation:** [guide](../../flagquantum/compiler/README.md)
- **Known boundary:** A count over a straight-line instruction sequence, and the basis is named in the record rather than left to the reader: ESTIMATE_BASIS is static_instruction_sequence. A program whose instruction sequence has data dependence is refused rather than estimated, and the refusal names the instruction index and the reason, so a rejected call reports why instead of returning a number for a program whose control flow was not modelled; the loop and function bounds that CUDA-Q estimates from the internal levels are therefore absent, because a straight-line instruction list has no loop bound to estimate and that needs ProgramIR. The depth is the compiler's own list schedule and not a latency: it is an as-soon-as-possible packing of the declared dependencies with no device timing, no gate duration, no connectivity, and no routing overhead, so it is a lower bound for a real device rather than a duration. No wall-clock time, resident memory, allocation, or fidelity is reported and none is measured. The counts are counts of one instruction sequence, which is the program as written unless the caller names a basis: passing `gates` converts the program first and the record's estimate_basis says which sequence was counted, so a T-count becomes available for a gate whose T-cost is its decomposition's -- under a label that names the basis, never under static_instruction_sequence. That conversion is a rewrite rather than a run, and a basis that cannot express the program exactly is refused rather than approximated, so no count is ever of an approximate sequence. The T family is the module's own table of t and tdg. Channels are counted rather than costed, and a noise model is not read. The unit executes nothing and adds no target, runtime option, result field, or execution mode, so no runtime or distributed semantics follow from this entry, and no Stable Core export follows either.


## Simulation and training

### Local statevector simulation and training

Run exact circuits and differentiable quantum workloads on a CPU or one GPU.

- **Maturity:** Production supported
- **Public API:** `fq.Circuit`, `fq.run`, `fq.Module`, `fq.train`
- **Runtime modes:** `statevector`
- **Hardware:** `cpu`, `single_gpu`
- **Gradient support:** `exact`
- **Distribution semantics:** `single_device_fast_path`
- **Start:** [quick example](../../examples/single_machine_quantum_ai/01_vqe_statevector.py)
- **Documentation:** [guide](../../examples/single_machine_quantum_ai/README.md)
- **Known boundary:** Capacity is bounded by one device; distributed capacity claims use the sharded capability. The paired complex64/complex128 CPU grid in artifacts/development/representation_agreement_precision/ covers open-boundary transverse-field Ising at h=0.7, widths 4-20, depths 2/4, nearest-neighbour throughout and all-to-all at widths 4/8, with bond caps 4/16 and identical float32-initialized angles. Each precision has 22 untruncated cells: maximum relative energy spread is 4.10343e-06 for complex64 and 1.10583e-14 for complex128. The six truncated cells per precision reach 0.225926 and 0.28199 respectively; higher precision does not eliminate truncation error. Wider all-to-all configurations are explicitly skipped because contraction repeats per Pauli term. These measured bounds do not cover arbitrary circuits or GPU execution. CI exercises a subgrid in both precisions. State, observable and parameter-gradient conformance is measured separately in artifacts/development/representation_conformance_cpu_20261028/: widths 4/8, depths 2/4, bond caps 4/16, nearest-neighbour throughout and all-to-all at width 8, three seeded draws (260720-260722) at both precisions, 48 cells per precision with 39 untruncated and 9 truncated. Where the cap does not bite, state fidelity and the parameter gradient agree to a maximum relative L2 error of 3.99256e-15 for MPS and 2.27939e-15 for the tensor-network path at complex128, and 3.60876e-06 and 1.23751e-06 at complex64, with cosine similarity at least 0.9999999999969. An independent parameter-shift oracle on the exact path, run only up to width 8, agrees to 2.92733e-15 and 1.67403e-06. Where the cap discards weight the gradient is reported rather than bounded: on the chain the truncated map stays smooth and autograd misses the derivative of its own map by 0.075-0.093, and on all-to-all the energy jumps by 0.507-2.623 between scan points 0.01 apart so no derivative exists there. The finite-difference check of the truncated map is valid only at complex128; at complex64 its 1e-5 step loses about six digits and reports up to 2.249 in cells where nothing was discarded. No recorded cell is near-degenerate, the smallest exact Schmidt-weight gap at a truncating cut being 1.23553e-08 against a 1e-09 threshold, and no derivative was refused. These bounds do not cover GPU or accelerator execution, widths above 8, or multi-step training semantics.

### Research capability discovery, execution, and evidence

Discover and execute the bounded local ground-state research slice through one transport-neutral service contract with preflight, scientific validation, and tamper-evident evidence.

- **Maturity:** Production supported
- **Public API:** `flagquantum.services.search_research_capabilities`, `flagquantum.services.describe_research_capability`, `flagquantum.services.estimate_research_capability`, `flagquantum.services.validate_research_capability_input`, `flagquantum.services.execute_research_capability`, `flagquantum.services.validate_research_evidence`, `flagquantum.services.list_research_schemas`, `flagquantum.services.describe_research_schema`
- **Runtime modes:** `statevector`
- **Hardware:** `cpu`
- **Gradient support:** `not_applicable`
- **Distribution semantics:** `single_device_fast_path`
- **Start:** [quick-start guide](../../docs/development/API_CHANGE_PROPOSAL_070_RESEARCH_CAPABILITY_SERVICES.md)
- **Documentation:** [guide](../../flagquantum/services/README.md)
- **Known boundary:** Approved namespace-scoped service contract at production-supported maturity for a bounded local CPU slice; it is not a Stable Core root API. A thin external adapter may call the public service, but FlagQuantum remains the only owner of its domain semantics. The executable scope is exactly three local CPU operations: two-qubit real Pauli-sum VQE in the fixed weight-one sector, exact diagonalization in that sector, and energy-variance validation of a normalized full statevector. Inputs use Hartree coefficients and wire_0_is_leftmost ordering. The VQE path uses the existing single-excitation ansatz and bounded Adam workflow; it is deterministic and records no random seed because this slice performs no stochastic sampling. VQE defaults to flagquantum-native and may explicitly select the optional local qiskit-aer or pennylane-lightning adapters. Multiple providers require separate fallback authorization; fallback advances only past an unknown or locally unavailable adapter, never an execution failure or scientific-validation failure, and all route assessments and attempts are content-hashed. Remote targets, QPUs, shot execution, noise models, broader Hamiltonians, alternate ansatzes, alternate optimizers, persistence, tenant control, natural-language routing, MCP dependencies, and every fallback axis outside that unavailable-only provider policy are unsupported. Evidence hashes detect accidental or untrusted mutation but are not signatures or runtime certification.

### One gradient entry point with a reported method

Differentiate a parameterized circuit through any exact execution mode without choosing an executor-specific route, and read which method produced the derivative.

- **Maturity:** Production supported
- **Public API:** `fq.gradient`, `fq.jacobian`, `fq.jvp`, `fq.vjp`
- **Runtime modes:** `statevector`, `mps`, `tensor_network`
- **Hardware:** `cpu`, `single_gpu`
- **Gradient support:** `exact`
- **Distribution semantics:** `single_device_fast_path`
- **Start:** [quick-start guide](../../examples/gradient_methods/README.md)
- **Documentation:** [guide](../../docs/reference/API.md)
- **Known boundary:** Only statevector, MPS, and tensor-network mode can serve the expectation value a differentiable program needs; stabilizer mode samples outcomes and fails closed instead. method='autograd' and method='parameter_shift' are exact; method='finite_difference' and method='spsa' are declared approximations whose result reports exact=False together with the displacement actually used. method='adjoint' is refused, because FlagQuantum has no standalone adjoint entry point: the reversible sweep is reachable only as the backward pass behind PyTorch autograd, and no result reports whether backward used adjoint replay. method='auto' measures the program rather than declaring a route, so it probes for an autograd graph before it resorts to a shift rule or a difference. The displacement method='finite_difference' defaults to is the cube root of the machine epsilon of the dtype the loss delivers, not of the dtype the parameters carry, so a program that casts inside its loss gets the step its own arithmetic justifies. fq.jacobian, fq.jvp, and fq.vjp exist for a program that returns several values, so that no single scalar gradient is defined; all three are autograd-only -- there is no shift rule for a vector output and no reportable displacement -- so a program whose output carries no PyTorch graph is refused rather than answered with a zero derivative. fq.jvp needs the program differentiated twice and is refused with CapabilityError where an executor cannot build a graph of a graph; fq.jacobian needs one backward pass per output element, and fq.vjp needs one backward pass in total whatever the parameter count. Every result is detached, so the derivative of a derivative requires an explicit second route rather than nesting these calls.

### Exact and trajectory-based noisy simulation

Write a channel into a circuit or lower a validated Kraus noise model into FlagQuantum IR, then execute exact density-matrix or MPS quantum-trajectory paths.

- **Maturity:** Experimental
- **Public API:** `flagquantum.noise.NoiseModel`, `flagquantum.noise.channel_from_parameters`, `flagquantum.noise.noisy_density_matrix`, `flagquantum.runtime.run_noisy_mps`, `flagquantum.runtime.run_noisy_statevector`
- **Runtime modes:** `density_matrix`, `noisy_mps`
- **Hardware:** `cpu`, `single_gpu`
- **Gradient support:** `unsupported`
- **Distribution semantics:** `single_device_fast_path_or_rank_local_trajectory_partition`
- **Start:** [quick example](../../examples/noisy_simulation_v1.py)
- **Documentation:** [guide](../../docs/guides/NOISY_SIMULATION.md)
- **Known boundary:** Validated Markovian Kraus channels, timestamped DeviceNoiseProfile input, ASAP gate/idle thermal lowering, classical readout confusion, exact density execution, and reproducible MPS trajectories with single-rank adaptive stopping are available. Four channel opcodes (bit_flip, phase_flip, depolarizing, amplitude_damping) declare the scalar their factory takes, so they can be written directly into a circuit as well as attached to a gate through a NoiseModel rule; the two routes lower to the same instruction, and a program is planned as noisy because it carries a channel, not because a model was passed. That same reading of the program decides the trajectory representation under mode='auto' and restores it from a saved plan, so an inline channel and an equivalent NoiseModel rule select the same route. A representation that holds amplitudes -- mps, tensor_network, and statevector -- refuses a channel instead of returning the noiseless number, and mode='stabilizer' names its own obstacle. A channel instruction's matrix field is the Kraus tuple, so consumers read it as a sequence rather than as one tensor. Only those four opcodes are reachable by name: the remaining flagquantum.noise callables, including thermal relaxation, readout error, and coherent overrotation, are NoiseModel rules only. Channel parameters are bound real scalars; a trainable channel probability has no gradient path. Lowering refuses a rule naming a qubit outside the program width and refuses a model whose rules match no instruction at all, so a misspelled gate name cannot yield a clean result; a model that matches some instructions stays legal. Pulse overlap, crosstalk, leakage, provider calibration adapters, distributed adaptive stopping, batched statevector trajectories, production multi-GPU scheduling, and noisy gradients remain unsupported. Multi-qubit MPS channels use an explicitly dense correctness fallback.

### Continuous-time Lindblad density-matrix evolution

Evolve small Markovian open systems on explicit time grids with physical collapse rates and time-resolved diagnostics.

- **Maturity:** Production supported
- **Public API:** `flagquantum.lindblad.run`, `flagquantum.lindblad.plan`, `flagquantum.lindblad.LindbladPlan`, `flagquantum.lindblad.amplitude_damping`, `flagquantum.lindblad.adjoint_gradient`
- **Runtime modes:** `continuous_time_density_matrix`
- **Hardware:** `cpu`
- **Gradient support:** `discretized_autograd_and_initial_density_matrix_adjoint`
- **Distribution semantics:** `single_device_fast_path`
- **Start:** [quick example](../../examples/lindblad_evolution.py)
- **Documentation:** [guide](../../flagquantum/simulation/README.md)
- **Known boundary:** The experimental RydbergProgram also enters the existing high-level CPU plan/run/adjoint path for one or two g/r/lost actors only. Simulation assembles C6/r^6 interactions at imposed linear-path interval midpoints and absorbing loss, reusing Core IR/scalar binding and the same Liouvillian, three integrators and adjoint. Native-only plan 1.7 seals actual Core source, normalized interval control bindings and numerical matrices, rejects inconsistent source even after rehashing, and executes captured numbers without source recompilation. tests/unit/test_rydberg_transport_contract.py preserves the baseline physical/gradient/optimizer/adjoint/replay consumer assertions with three native fixtures; tests/unit/test_native_rydberg_dynamics.py covers native input consistency and strict source-aware replay. examples/rydberg_native_dynamics.py is the runnable pair example. This experimental bounded input profile does not promote the frontend to Stable Core or certify full M3: no sampled events, feedback, heating, quantum motion, GPU/distributed atom dynamics or concrete neutral-atom cloud adapter is supplied. Ordered basis_labels on the existing entries declare non-factorized matrix coordinates, mutually exclusive with dimensions/n_qubits. The internal Core ExplicitBasisSpace preserves exact ordered integer/string labels without inferring tensor factors, including at dimension two or four. Labelled static and piecewise requests use plan 1.6 with null n_wires, no product dimensions, retained request labels and an exactly revalidated decision copy; plan.n_qubits and plan.dimensions refuse this path. Full numeric states, matrices and existing scalar coefficient terms reuse the same Core binder, validation, Liouvillian, three integrators, shared trajectory, result and initial-density adjoint. Live gradients, shared initial batches, optimizer updates, strict numeric replay and missing-graph refusals remain bounded by the existing numerical limits. test_explicit_basis_contract.py replaces the test basis/numerical factories without changing consumer assertions; test_lindblad_explicit_basis.py checks vacuum-plus-one-photon loss against the ambient model and analytic laws, serialization/refusals, old plan fixtures, independent dense initial-adjoint references and product/labelled graph parity. examples/constrained_basis_reference.py retains literal photon/Ising basis labels through real plan/run. The caller owns orthonormality, model context, embeddings and invariance; labels do not certify a physical sector or define native fusion/braiding. Direct BosonOperator conversion still needs product dimensions; labelled models supply validated matrices. Explicit ordered finite subsystem dimensions are supported through dimensions= on the existing CPU path. Non-qubit spaces accept full numeric states and dense operator matrices, with subsystem 0 most significant; Pauli forms, bitstrings and named qubit collapse operators remain qubit-only even when the total dimension is a power of two. Static non-qubit sealed plans use schema 1.4, while static qubit plans retain 1.3 content and identities. Piecewise-constant full-space Hamiltonian terms accept parameter_values with exactly one complete Core parameter binding per output interval; all initial-state batch members share that control sequence. Scheduled plans use 1.5 and store ordered numerical Hamiltonians once, retaining live coefficient graphs but not serializing them. The same trajectory loop, Liouvillian and three integrators run each frozen interval, and initial-density adjoints reverse the matching interval generators. tests/unit/test_piecewise_control_contract.py replaces a static-composition fake with the public scheduled run; tests/unit/test_lindblad_piecewise_controls.py and examples/train_piecewise_drive.py check physical order, nonuniform grids, independent dense references, gradient/optimizer updates, unchanged static identities, JSON replay and early refusals. Interval matrices additionally cost N*D*D*element_bytes, shared across batches; trajectory_bytes estimates only density history, not total memory. The mixed-space workflow, analytic populations/decay and coupling gradients, initial-density adjoints, serialization, batch axes, truncation and time-grid refinement are covered by tests/unit/test_lindblad_mixed_dimensions.py and examples/mixed_dimension_dynamics.py. This is finite-dimensional matrix dynamics, not a certified fusion/anyon model, a native qudit circuit instruction set or a photonic backend. Static dense or matrix-free Pauli Hamiltonians and scheduled full-space operator terms, finite strictly increasing grids, Markovian Lindblad collapse operators, and CPU complex64/complex128 execution only. The integrator is selectable between explicit fourth-order Runge-Kutta, the second-order implicit Crank-Nicolson trapezoidal rule and a matrix-free exponential scheme. The two order-p schemes each take two substeps per output interval; the exponential scheme takes the interval whole, because the exponential of a time-independent generator is exact on the grid, so it reports no order and its accuracy is set by solve_tolerance instead. Both Krylov schemes apply the generator rather than forming it: Crank-Nicolson through restarted GMRES, the exponential through a single Arnoldi window whose projected exponential is the only exponential computed, so neither allocates the 16-to-the-n vectorized generator. The relative residual or defect target defaults to 1e-12 in complex128 and 1e-5 in complex64, it is refused for the explicit scheme rather than ignored, and a step that misses the target fails the evolution as integration_not_converged. For reference and for the properties that are statements about it, Liouvillian materializes the vectorized generator on demand but refuses to above a declared 16 MiB ceiling, which admits five wires and refuses six. A state may carry a batch axis -- a batch of statevectors as (batch, dim) or a batch of density matrices as (batch, dim, dim) -- with (dim, dim) keeping its reading as one density matrix; batch_size is refused when it contradicts the state, and the batch axis is folded into the solved Krylov vector rather than looping over members. The integrators are ordinary differentiable tensor operations, so a trajectory parameterized through either the Hamiltonian or the initial state carries an exact autograd graph, and the matrix-free solves keep the norms that scale their basis and result on that graph; require_gradients=True is decided on the returned trajectory and a detached one is refused as gradients_not_available. The gradient is that of the discretized evolution, not of the exact solution. flagquantum.lindblad.adjoint_gradient additionally returns a reverse-mode cotangent through that same discrete scheme: the forward sweep retains the output trajectory under no_grad and the reverse sweep applies the Hilbert-Schmidt adjoint of the vectorized generator, so no forward graph is kept and the cotangent is the conjugate transpose of the scheme the caller named rather than of the exact solution. The reverse map is the Hilbert-Schmidt adjoint of the generator that was assembled, `SuperOperator.adjoint()`, so the forward and reverse maps are one term list read in two directions; it is verified against the Liouvillian's own dense conjugate transpose and by the pairing identity between the two, and a matrix-free Pauli-sum Hamiltonian is reversed without being materialized, because `PauliSum.adjoint()` conjugates its coefficients and leaves the Hermitian strings alone. The route refuses require_gradients=True, a cost that is not callable, and a cost that does not return a scalar, and its cotangent follows the complex-gradient convention in which a perturbation pairs with it through the real part of the inner product of its conjugate with that perturbation. One measured property of the forward path is disclosed here rather than repaired: at a base point where the two routes differ most, the adjoint route reproduces the dense transpose of the scheme it was given to roundoff, while the backward pass through the matrix-free solve is an approximation of that transpose -- relative to a five-point stencil of the same scheme it is 3.5e-06 away for Crank-Nicolson and 1.7e-05 for the exponential scheme, where the adjoint route is 1.0e-10 and 8.9e-11 away, and a finite difference of one Crank-Nicolson interval agrees with that scheme's dense transpose to 4.6e-11 where autograd's cotangent is 5.7e-08 away from it. The explicit scheme, which takes no solve, is at roundoff on both. That gap is the conditioning of the discretized generator rather than a defective vector-Jacobian product, because the matrix-free solve's own backward is exact to 1.7e-15 on a well-conditioned system. Callable or interpolated controls, scheduled Pauli descriptors, time-dependent collapse rates, differentiable time boundaries, non-Markovian environments, stochastic trajectories, sparse solvers, GPU/distributed execution, hardware submission, and arbitrary time-dependent generators are unsupported. Exactly closed Krylov subspaces preserve forward value bits and now receive a first-order residual-sensitivity correction through the same existing matrix-free solver; the exponential correction uses an augmented n+m vector rather than a dense generator. tests/unit/test_matrix_free_closed_subspace_gradients.py checks transverse operator/input derivatives, zero arguments and failure when the same Krylov budget is insufficient. Higher-order differentiation through this closed-subspace correction is explicitly refused; no Hessian capability is claimed. The earlier conditioning observations for non-closed solves remain separate. A local Krylov defect target is not a global population-error guarantee: the piecewise refinement witness observed a projected-exponential numerical floor around 1.37e-11 despite a 1e-14 local target, so global error is tested separately without relaxing existing solver regressions. Integrator selection is covered by tests/unit/test_lindblad_integrators.py, the vectorized generator and exponential step by tests/unit/test_lindblad_vectorized_generator.py, and the batch axis and gradient path by tests/unit/test_lindblad_batching_and_gradients.py. The adjoint cotangent, its agreement with the dense transpose of each scheme and its retention claim are covered by tests/unit/test_lindblad_adjoint_gradient.py. The superoperator algebra beside this route reads a map's Choi matrix through `flagquantum.operators.SuperOperator.choi`, in the index order `J[(a, i), (b, j)] = E(E_ab)[i, j]`, and certifies complete positivity through `is_completely_positive`, which is the eigenvalue test that separates amplitude damping or dephasing from the transpose map; that certificate reads its tolerance relative to the Choi matrix's own spectral scale because the absolute rounding error grows with the dimension and the entries, reaching 1.337e+06 epsilons in complex64 and 9.956e+05 in complex128 on a rescaled channel whose smallest eigenvalue is exactly zero. A map that does not preserve Hermiticity -- an anti-Hermitian generator, for instance -- is refused by name rather than reported as not completely positive. Both normalization conditions are public accessors rather than recipes: `SuperOperator.partial_trace` sums one of the two registers of the Choi index order, so its `input` register is `E(I)` -- the identity exactly for a unital map -- and its `output` register is the identity exactly for a trace-preserving one. The two are separated by a map that is trace preserving and not unital, which is what amplitude damping is, and the accessor adds no arithmetic of its own: it is the same sum the owning suite measured by hand, now named and refused by name for a register that is neither. The algebra also reads a Kraus list back off that matrix rather than only accepting one: `SuperOperator.kraus` diagonalizes the Choi matrix and weights each eigenvector by the square root of its eigenvalue, so a channel that arrived as a matrix, a term list or a Lindblad route leaves as a Kraus list, and three operators spanning two dimensions come back as two. It is the count and the map that are pinned rather than the individual operators, because the eigenvector phases belong to the eigenproblem; the unstacking index order is not free, and dropping its transpose is measured 2.625e-01 away from the map's own action on amplitude damping at gamma = 0.4, where the `[0, 0]` entry of that candidate is exactly `-gamma * rho[1, 1]`. The round trip through `from_kraus` returns the map to 5.594e-17 at two levels and 1.665e-16 at three, and to the worst of 1.788e-07 over every fixture in complex64, which is the dtype's rounding rather than the decomposition's. A map whose Choi matrix has a negative eigenvalue is refused by name with that eigenvalue, because no Kraus list denotes it and the closest one would be a different map -- the transpose map's is exactly -1.000000 against a scale of exactly 1.000000. The byte ceiling, the Hermiticity refusal and the tolerance are the choi certificate's rather than a second set, so the two methods answer from one eigenproblem and one rule. The experimental IsingBraidProgram follows the existing fq.plan/run path only for four sigma anyons with fixed total vacuum charge, the reviewed fusion tree/gauge and static CPU complex128 matrix outputs. Native-only plans use 1.1 for braids, 1.2 for one ideal unread charge observation and 1.3 for one ideal sampled charge trajectory, sealing matching Core source, ordered output tensors, resolved options and shared environment/compiler fingerprints; circuit plans 1.0 and Lindblad plans 1.3-1.7 retain their captured bytes. Sampled 1.3 requires explicit shots=1 and seed, seals RNG Torch version in the existing environment protocol 1.1, returns a conditional density plus one existing mid_circuit_measurement record, and rejects a different Torch build before a draw. Its 216-byte numeric subtotal is not a process-RSS bound; state storage remains 64 bytes. tests/unit/test_ising_sampled_contract.py retains independent conditional-density and weighted-unread assertions after replacing four factories with the real native paths; tests/integration/test_ising_sampled_execution.py covers saved public execution without replanning. Native analysis is null rather than an encoded-qubit substitute. Simulation applies finite F/R actions through existing SuperOperator algebra, with no elapsed time, Hamiltonian or integrator. The four baseline factories now use the real source/planner/executor/reader while their independent physical assertions remain unchanged; examples/ising_braid_execution.py demonstrates exact JSON replay and explicit terminal charge QND sampling. Gradients, approximate/backend fallback, general multi-shot native execution, feedback, remote execution, calibration, other anyon sectors/models, GPU/distributed braiding and physical hardware remain refused. Lindblad still rejects the discrete source before Rydberg assembly; this bounded path does not certify full M3 or universal anyon computation.

### Superoperator algebra over left and right multiplication terms

State a linear map on matrices as a sum of `coefficient * left @ state @ right` terms, then apply it, densify it, read it in any orthonormal matrix basis, place it on part of a register, and reverse it with its Hilbert--Schmidt adjoint.

- **Maturity:** Production supported
- **Public API:** `flagquantum.operators`, `flagquantum.operators.SuperOperator`, `flagquantum.operators.SuperOperator.from_kraus`, `flagquantum.operators.SuperOperator.left_multiply`, `flagquantum.operators.SuperOperator.right_multiply`, `flagquantum.operators.SuperOperator.left_right_multiply`, `flagquantum.operators.SuperOperator.apply`, `flagquantum.operators.SuperOperator.apply_to_subsystem`, `flagquantum.operators.SuperOperator.dense`, `flagquantum.operators.SuperOperator.terms`, `flagquantum.operators.SuperOperator.matrix_in_basis`, `flagquantum.operators.SuperOperator.adjoint`, `flagquantum.operators.SuperOperator.choi`, `flagquantum.operators.SuperOperator.is_completely_positive`, `flagquantum.operators.SuperOperator.partial_trace`, `flagquantum.operators.SuperOperator.kraus`
- **Runtime modes:** `not_applicable`
- **Hardware:** `cpu`
- **Gradient support:** `exact`
- **Distribution semantics:** `single_device_primitive_only`
- **Start:** [quick example](../../examples/superoperator_channel_algebra.py)
- **Documentation:** [guide](../../flagquantum/operators/README.md)
- **Known boundary:** A map enters the algebra only as a term list: an accumulator that starts empty, `from_kraus` over a Kraus list, or one of the three multiply constructors. The accumulator is not a no-action map -- it carries no dimension and no dtype until a term is added, and both `dimension` and `apply` refuse it by name rather than acting as the identity. There is no constructor from a dense matrix or from a Pauli transfer matrix, so a channel known only as a 4**k matrix cannot enter the algebra, and a transfer matrix can be read out but not read back in. `dense` materializes the vectorized map in the repository-wide row-major vectorization vec(A rho B) = (A kron B^T) vec(rho), where the right factor is transposed and never conjugate-transposed, and it refuses above a 16 MiB ceiling computed as entries times element bytes: at complex128 a 32-dimensional map is 1024 x 1024 with 1048576 entries and is materialized at exactly 16777216 bytes, while a 64-dimensional one is 4096 x 4096 with 16777216 entries, 268435456 bytes, and is refused by name with that ceiling and the advice to apply the map instead. `apply_to_subsystem` widens each factor onto the named qubits through `flagquantum.simulation.density_matrix.expand_operator`, costs the state rather than a d**4 object, and needs no ceiling of its own, which is what makes the ceiling a property of `dense` rather than of the algebra: the 64-dimensional map measured above, refused as a 268435456-byte matrix, is applied to qubits 0--5 of a seven-qubit register in the same run and returns a 128 x 128 state whose trace is 0.9700000000000001, the sum of the squared singular values of its two Kraus operators. A factor is a `torch.Tensor` or a `PauliSum`: `apply` evaluates each term as a matrix product and so keeps a non-tensor factor matrix-free, while widening it onto a subsystem and densifying the map both go through its own `dense()`, and there is no sparse factor and no batched-factor form. The four certificate accessors -- `choi`, `is_completely_positive`, `partial_trace` and `kraus` -- answer from one eigenproblem of the Choi matrix under one rule: a tolerance relative to that matrix's own scale, measured 1.776e-15 at unit scale, rather than an absolute number. `is_completely_positive` reports a bool while `kraus` raises on the same matrix, because the two callers ask different questions: a bool leaves the caller free to act on the answer, while a Kraus list has to denote *this* map, and the closest positive one would be a different map returned silently. Two refusals precede that test rather than being folded into it -- a map that does not preserve Hermiticity is reported as such instead of as not completely positive, since a generator has no positivity to report -- and `continuous_time_lindblad` records both refusals in the words the route that consumes them needs; this entry states the rule and does not copy their text. The algebra performs no time evolution, chooses no integrator, allocates no plan, and reports no provenance: `flagquantum.simulation.lindblad_generator` owns the forward generator and derives its reverse map from `adjoint`, and `flagquantum.algorithms.pec` owns the Pauli twirl that reads `matrix_in_basis`. Gradients are ordinary autograd through the coefficients and the factors, with no gradient rule of the algebra's own: at damping probability 0.4 the derivative of the damped excited-state population with respect to that probability is exactly minus the incoming excited-state population. `apply` accepts a leading batch axis and maps row by row; there is no sharded, distributed or multi-device path, and while the algebra runs on whatever device the caller's tensors occupy, the focused and integration suites exercise CPU only.

### Repetition-code memory experiment

Run a bounded three-data-qubit memory experiment with timed errors or circuit-location bit-flip/readout noise, compiled feedback, per-round Runtime decoding, or Pauli-frame correction, including a two-round temporal reference decoder.

- **Maturity:** Development evidence
- **Public API:** `flagquantum.qec.run_repetition_memory_experiment`, `flagquantum.qec.run_repetition_memory_noise_sweep`, `flagquantum.qec.RepetitionNoiseProfile`, `flagquantum.qec.ErrorSchedule`, `flagquantum.qec.Decoder`, `flagquantum.qec.StreamingDecoder`, `flagquantum.qec.RepetitionTemporalDecoder`
- **Runtime modes:** `local_statevector_compiled_feedback`, `local_statevector_runtime_decoder`, `local_statevector_runtime_pauli_frame`, `local_statevector_runtime_temporal_decoder`, `local_statevector_runtime_temporal_pauli_frame`, `local_statevector_offline_pauli_frame`, `local_statevector_noisy_trajectory`
- **Hardware:** `cpu`
- **Gradient support:** `unsupported`
- **Distribution semantics:** `single_process`
- **Start:** [quick-start guide](../../flagquantum/qec/README.md)
- **Documentation:** [guide](../../flagquantum/qec/README.md)
- **Known boundary:** A synchronous local reference for one fixed three-data-qubit repetition-code profile. It supports bounded deterministic X-error schedules, replaceable per-round trajectory decoding with physical-X or Pauli-frame-X actions, and a two-round temporal rule that rejects an isolated readout excursion. Confirmable data errors require a following round; terminal-round onsets remain unconfirmed. The circuit-location stochastic profile contains independent bit flips after parity-check CNOTs and independent syndrome/final-readout confusion. The middle data qubit has two CNOT noise opportunities per round while edge qubits have one. Feedback traces separate true and observed bits, actions, and frame evolution. Sweeps report finite-shot observations only, not logical suppression or thresholds. The temporal rule is not maximum-likelihood decoding and repeated readout faults may mimic data errors. Batched decoder feedback, general channels/codes, correlated or timing noise, hard-real-time/provider control, gradients, distributed execution, capacity, performance, and fault-tolerance claims remain unsupported. The feedback records are private subinterfaces and the namespace is not exported from the stable package root.

### Stabilizer-code memory circuit

Declare a stabilizer code record -- directly, by name, or from the parity-check and logical matrices a caller already holds -- and turn it into a memory-experiment source with the detector and observable layouts that experiment implies, for the repetition code, the rotated surface code and the Steane code.

- **Maturity:** Development evidence
- **Public API:** `flagquantum.qec.build_memory_circuit`, `flagquantum.qec.RepetitionCode`, `flagquantum.qec.RotatedSurfaceCode`, `flagquantum.qec.SteaneCode`, `flagquantum.qec.css_code_matrices`, `flagquantum.qec.CssCodeMatrices`, `flagquantum.qec.CssCode`, `flagquantum.qec.CodeCheck`, `flagquantum.qec.Pauli`, `flagquantum.qec.MemoryCircuit`, `flagquantum.qec.DetectorLayout`, `flagquantum.qec.ObservableLayout`, `flagquantum.qec.MeasurementRef`, `flagquantum.qec.MeasurementSamples`, `flagquantum.qec.sample_memory_measurements`, `flagquantum.qec.ancilla_bands`, `flagquantum.qec.get_code`, `flagquantum.qec.code_names`, `flagquantum.qec.register_code`
- **Runtime modes:** `local_statevector_dynamic_hybrid`
- **Hardware:** `cpu`
- **Gradient support:** `unsupported`
- **Distribution semantics:** `single_process`
- **Start:** [quick-start guide](../../flagquantum/qec/README.md)
- **Documentation:** [guide](../../flagquantum/qec/IMPLEMENTATION.md)
- **Known boundary:** A code-independent declaration layer, not a decoder and not an experiment runner. build_memory_circuit emits bounded hybrid source text plus detector and observable layouts; executing that source is the Runtime's dynamic hybrid path and carries that path's own limits. Three code records are registered by name: the repetition code, the rotated surface code and the Steane code, the last of which is the first record carrying both a Z-type and an X-type logical observable. The package ships more records than the registry names -- the colour, toric, bivariate-bicycle, punctured Reed-Muller, ZXXZ-surface, tesseract and ring Floquet records are declared without a registry name -- so code_names is the registry's roster rather than a count of this layer's records. Each record is reachable by name through get_code, listed by code_names and added with register_code, which checks the protocol members at registration and refuses a duplicate name unless replace is set; the names are repetition, rotated_surface and steane, and the registry is the factory half of upstream's get_code and get_available_codes rather than a plugin boundary. A record declares its ancilla total and also the X-type and Z-type bands, and num_x_stabilizers and num_z_stabilizers beside them, which is the full accessor list upstream's code record carries as pure virtuals; the two bands are derived from the checks by ancilla_bands rather than declared beside them, and the two stabilizer counts read those same bands rather than recounting the checks, because each of the three code classes upstream ships answers the same number to a stabilizer count as to the band count of that basis: the builder refuses a record whose stated counts disagree with the bands its own checks define, so a record cannot report a split that contradicts it, while a flag or idle ancilla measures neither basis and is in neither band, leaving the two bands free not to cover the total. The colour code is among the records now, and it arrived after the survey this paragraph records: triangular_colour_code derives the triangular patch, and its two check families are literally one matrix, so the patch is self-dual. That survey recorded that upstream's headers declare no colour code and no concrete code type at all, only the abstract code interface and the three families its own factory names, Steane, repetition and surface, of which this repository carries one of each; the reading of those headers is the survey's and is not re-measured here. A check states one ancilla and two directed runs -- the Z-factor pairs first, then the X-factor pairs, with the H pair separating them -- so a mixed X-and-Z stabilizer is measurable by one ancilla and needs no second, and the earlier reading that refused one is retracted rather than left standing. A check whose second run is empty is the pure case, so the two gadgets are the ends of one rule instead of two rules. The readout basis is a field of the record and defaults to the Z one, so one code record can describe two experiments: the basis states which of the code's logical observables the terminal readout measures, and it is checked against the observables the code declares rather than assumed from them, so a code declaring no logical observable in the requested basis is refused rather than measured under premises that do not hold for it. Upstream derives the same basis from the preparation kernel it is handed, where here it is stated on the record, because the Steane code declares a logical observable of each type and the code alone would not say which of the two experiments was built. Upstream's separate x_ and z_ model entry points stay absent deliberately, because a basis is a component of the record here rather than a second way to ask for one thing. Detector counts follow the two check classes, the readout basis' own class contributing rounds + 1 and the other rounds - 1 -- z_checks * (rounds + 1) + x_checks * (rounds - 1) for the default Z basis -- and are validated against the code's checks and the configured rounds, but the validation is membership-based: it does not check that a detector names the same check in every round, that a terminal detector's data qubits are the support of the check it belongs to, or that the emitted source is the program the layouts describe, so a hand-built layout can be semantically wrong while passing every check. A detection event is a parity of recorded measurements, and the record is readable at the level underneath it: MemoryCircuit.measurement_refs is every measurement the experiment records -- one handle per check per round in round-major order, then one per data qubit for the terminal readout -- read off the code and the round count rather than collected from the layouts, and sample_memory_measurements returns those outcomes with MeasurementSamples.outcome, .vector and .integer reading one handle, a chosen sub-vector as booleans in the order asked for, and that same order packed with the first handle least significant. The two sets need not agree in either direction, and the reason the handle layer exists is the direction where the layouts are right to be smaller: round zero of a patch measures the check class its preparation does not stabilize -- an X-type check under the all-zero preparation, a Z-type check under the |+> one -- whose outcome is not deterministic, so no detector may name it, and it is still measured and still readable. A detector's parity recomputed from the handles that detector names equals the detector's parity shot for shot, which the tests assert per detector and per observable rather than assuming. The layer makes no claim about which handles are deterministic, and a test measures the border instead of stating it: a noiseless run reads the readout basis' own check class' ancilla as 0 in every round and the other class' ancilla as unbiased, the two swapping places under an X-basis preparation, and an individual terminal data qubit as unbiased, while every detector and every observable reads 0. A rate read off a single handle is therefore a rate read off the state and not a channel rate, and a detector is a parity and not a measurement. A reading that does not fit is refused rather than approximated: a handle the experiment does not record raises naming the handle, an empty reading raises, a reading addressed by anything but a MeasurementRef raises as a type error, and a record whose tensor is not two-dimensional or whose column count does not match its handle vector raises rather than being truncated. What the layer is not is a kernel annotation form -- detector, detectors and logical_observable as kernel calls are not implemented here, and identity is set in a layout record beside the source rather than in a kernel body the compiler would have to carry into IR. A code also does not have to be declared as a class: a caller holding the four CSS blocks -- hz, hx, lz and lx -- builds a code record out of them, and the data-qubit count, the ancilla total with its two bands, the checks with their CNOT direction, the stabilizers and the logical observables are read off those blocks, so an arbitrary parity-check matrix has a route in without a class for it and a code no class here declares, the nine-qubit Shor code for instance, reaches a memory circuit and a detector error model. Two things about that route are deliberate. Its distance is stated by the caller rather than derived, because a minimum-weight-codeword search is exponential in general and would refuse exactly the large matrices the route exists for, while the one direction a stated logical operator does prove is enforced: an operator of weight w bounds the distance above by w, so a record claiming more than the lightest operator it states is refused and one that understates its distance is accepted. And the blocks are held to the algebra a matrix record normally leaves unchecked: two check blocks that do not commute, a logical operator meeting the opposite basis' checks on an odd number of qubits, a logical operator inside its own checks' span, two dependent rows in one logical block, and a check or logical row with no support are each refused with the row and the reason named, which is a strengthening of the baseline rather than a copy of it. What the route still does not carry is identity: a record built this way is not registered by name, because its identity is the matrix rather than a string, so get_code reaches the declared three and not a matrix-built record. Nor is a code here a map from an operation to the kernel that performs it, which is upstream's shape: a record declares its checks and one source is built from them, so a code carrying its own preparation, measurement or logical-operation kernels would need a second shape, and an arbitrary non-CSS stabilizer list has no route in for the same reason a mixed X-and-Z stabilizer is refused, because the matrices route is CSS-shaped by construction. The rotated surface code's declared logical observable is Z on one data row; which weight-d logical operator a patch uses is a convention, and this record states the one it uses rather than deriving all of them. Agreement with stim's generated rotated-memory circuit is on the declared detector and observable count at distances two through eight and rounds one through four, which fixes the shape and not a mechanism-level correspondence. Execution evidence covers the distance-three patch only, because the dynamic hybrid simulator draws each shot from the full output distribution and a distance-five patch needs 2**49 categories. No decoding, no threshold, no logical-suppression, no fault-tolerance, no real-time and no hardware-feedback claim follows from a declared code, a detector layout, or a syndrome trace.

### Detector error model and stim text interchange

Build the exact Pauli-noise detector error model of a memory circuit or of a code-capacity CSS record, sample it, and exchange independent models with stim as text in both directions.

- **Maturity:** Development evidence
- **Public API:** `flagquantum.qec.DetectorErrorModel`, `flagquantum.qec.DemError`, `flagquantum.qec.DemMergeRule`, `flagquantum.qec.DemSample`, `flagquantum.qec.css_code_matrices`, `flagquantum.qec.decoder_context_from_memory_circuit`, `flagquantum.qec.DecoderContext`, `flagquantum.qec.DecoderInputs`, `flagquantum.qec.MeasurementMap`
- **Runtime modes:** `not_applicable`
- **Hardware:** `cpu`
- **Gradient support:** `unsupported`
- **Distribution semantics:** `single_process`
- **Start:** [quick-start guide](../../flagquantum/qec/IMPLEMENTATION.md)
- **Documentation:** [guide](../../flagquantum/qec/IMPLEMENTATION.md)
- **Known boundary:** An exact construction for Pauli noise over the reference Clifford gate set, and a text reader for the stim detector error model format. Production construction does not sample or allocate a dense statevector. Round-boundary mechanisms are derived from the declared check and measurement layouts. Gate-bound bit-flip and phase-flip mechanisms are enumerated at their existing single-wire locations and read through exact backwards Clifford parity propagation in Simulation, including measurement records, resets and terminal readout. QEC-FUNCTIONAL-004 extends gate-bound construction to one-/two-qubit Pauli Kraus mixtures, including biased mixtures, Y flip and depolarization, using existing Noise probabilities and Simulation Pauli recognition. Each matched instruction/rule/target location is independent of other locations; competing products at one location share an error_id and are categorical alternatives. Equal signatures at one location sum their probabilities; silent branches remain no-effect mass without renormalization. Independent exclusive locations are not collapsed by parity merging. The original X/Z-only mechanisms and seeded sampling stream remain unchanged. The existing Clifford images are reused, and a nondeterministic parity or unsupported instruction is refused rather than approximated. The forced two-trajectory execution remains the independent small-system reference: agreement concerns detector/observable flip sets, not the random raw ancilla register. QEC-FUNCTIONAL-003 compares every enumerated location and the merged model against that reference for repetition, rotated, Steane and mixed ZXXZ circuits, including X readout where the code declares it. Circuit-location surface models now build at distances five and seven (49 and 97 physical qubits) with the reference execution disabled; local CPU construction counts and allocation measurements for distances three, five and seven are recorded in benchmarks/results/local/qec_clifford_dem_cpu_arm64_20261024.json. This is construction capacity evidence, not decoder accuracy, logical suppression, threshold, accelerator, realtime or distributed scaling evidence; the maturity tier remains development evidence. Historical forced-execution measurements are retained for the reference route: distance two is seven qubits and distance three is seventeen, distance four is thirty-one qubits and did not complete in forty-five minutes, and distance five is forty-nine qubits and failed on the allocator. Those historical statevector ceilings no longer limit production circuit-location construction. A second construction route reads a code-capacity experiment rather than a memory circuit. DetectorErrorModel.from_code_matrices takes a CssCodeMatrices record -- the four CSS blocks hz, hx, lz and lx -- and derives every mechanism combinatorially, and css_code_matrices lifts a code record into that record, refusing a logical observable that is neither pure X nor pure Z rather than half-reading a CSS code. Its geometry is not the memory circuit's and the difference is pinned rather than glossed: a fault in round r reaches the detector band of round r and of round r + 1, the final round has no band after it, and there is no terminal data readout, so the detector count is num_rounds * num_checks where a memory circuit gains one terminal detector per check of the readout basis it is built in. Both geometries are asserted against their own stim transcription, and the same suite asserts that the two counts differ. The matrix route is compared to stim one readout basis at a time, because lz and lx anti-commute and no one state has both as a deterministic value: the reference experiment is run twice against the model restricted to the matching basis, and each run's observable rows are the ones the Pauli character of that fault family predicts. A fault that is still in the data at the end of the run is still seen by the logical readout, so a physical fault spans one detector band while the model gives it two; that single divergence is pinned as the exact relation between the two mechanism sets rather than as a tolerance.
