Accepted gates are exactly the thirteen Clifford opcodes in flagquantum.core.operator_schema -- i, x, y, z, h, s, sdg, sx, sxdg, cx, cy, cz, swap -- and every other opcode is refused with a CapabilityError naming it and the accepted set. ccx and cswap are refused because neither normalizes the Pauli group, so they are not Clifford gates despite being permutations; t, tdg, the rotation and controlled-rotation family, phase, u1, u2 and u3 are refused for the same reason. Circuits passed to sample_stabilizer must be noiseless: the four noise channels are refused rather than sampled as unitaries, so no readout error, depolarizing, damping or trajectory behavior is available. The second entry point, sample_noisy_measurements, is the narrow exception, and it is not a widening of that refusal: it executes a bit-flip channel at an explicit position a caller has already chosen, so the frame tracking is the caller's placement and not an inference the engine makes, and a channel whose Kraus operators are not the two-element bit-flip pair is still refused by name. It reads no lowered measurement node, returns only the sampled bits with no detection events or observable flips, and is not reachable from the planner or the executor, so `mode='stabilizer'` keeps refusing a noisy program. Only computational-basis measurement sampling is supported; expectation values, detector error models, decoding, error-corrected logical operations and hardware-backed sampling are absent. Sampling is not differentiable and returns no gradients. The execution route is explicit: `fq.ExecutionOptions(mode='stabilizer')` reaches the engine through `fq.run`, the planner publishes a plan whose `state_mode` is `stabilizer` and whose `state_bytes` is the packed tableau rather than an amplitude count, and the result reports `execution_path='local_stabilizer'`. Automatic selection never chooses this mode, so no existing plan changes meaning. Planning refuses a non-Clifford gate, a noise channel, a batch, a non-CPU device, a gradient request, a non-sampling target, a sharded world size and a program without shots; sampling refuses a lowered request it cannot reconcile with its own arguments and postselection, and a memory limit below the tableau. None of those refusals is a fallback, and none of them is a scalability claim. Runs are CPU-only single-device work with no GPU or distributed sharding. A seed reproduces a run on one engine version and one machine instruction set; it does not pin a bit pattern across machines or engine versions. The capacity statement is representational -- memory grows with the qubit count squared rather than exponentially -- and no throughput, latency or scaling claim is made.

### Reversible integer addition

Build the in-place ripple-carry adder that writes the sum of two n-bit registers into the second of them on one working carry wire, and read its register map so a caller knows which wire carries what.

- **Maturity:** Development evidence
- **Public API:** `flagquantum.algorithms.arithmetic`, `flagquantum.algorithms.arithmetic.adder_circuit`, `flagquantum.algorithms.arithmetic.adder_qubits`, `flagquantum.algorithms.arithmetic.AdderQubits`
- **Runtime modes:** `local_statevector`
- **Hardware:** `cpu`
- **Gradient support:** `not_applicable`
- **Distribution semantics:** `single_device_fast_path`
- **Start:** [quick example](../../examples/algorithms/arithmetic.py)
- **Documentation:** [guide](../../docs/guides/ALGORITHMS.md)
- **Known boundary:** A circuit constructor, and no arithmetic beyond it. Both addends are quantum registers, so there is no classical addend and no add_constant entry point, no modular adder, no controlled adder, no comparison, no multiplier, and no modular exponentiation, and the adder is not a reversible-arithmetic library. This is Cuccaro's ripple, not the Gidney-Ekera construction: it is 2 n Toffolis and 6 n + 1 cx for 8 n + 1 operations on 2 n + 2 wires, and Gidney-Ekera is smaller because it uncomputes its ancillas by measuring them and feeding the outcomes forward, which needs a mid-circuit measurement and a classical feedforward path this repository does not have in a circuit it can cost statically. Two wires must enter holding |0>: the working carry wire, which the circuit restores, and the carry-out wire, which it leaves holding the carry out of the most significant position; only inputs meeting that contract are promised a sum, and the circuit is a permutation of the whole space rather than only of those inputs, so an off-contract input is reversible and is not the sum. The carry-in is not a parameter: the ripple reads no incoming carry, so a caller wanting one has to add it before or after rather than inside. This module builds the construction and does not cost it. The T-count is the compiler's: ccx lowers to the fifteen-gate h, t, tdg, cx identity in flagquantum.compiler.basis_translation, so the adder is priced by convert_basis plus flagquantum.compiler.resource_estimation.estimate_resources, and flagquantum.algorithms.logical_resources passes that tally through. No seven-T expansion is written here, because a second Toffoli cost would be a second source of truth for the one number the two must agree on. No distillation factory, magic-state budget, routing overhead, or layout is included, so any surface-code figure a caller derives from this circuit is a floor rather than a compiled estimate. The circuit is not executed, selects no runtime, and carries no simulator; it adds no target, runtime option, result field, or execution mode, and no Stable Core export follows from this entry.

### Block encoding of a Hermitian matrix, and its qubitization walk step

Encode a Hermitian matrix into the flagged block of a unitary at a subnormalisation the caller chooses, and append one qubitization walk step whose eigenphases are the arccosines of the encoded eigenvalues.

- **Maturity:** Development evidence
- **Public API:** `flagquantum.algorithms.primitives.block_encoding`, `flagquantum.algorithms.primitives.BlockEncoding`, `flagquantum.algorithms.primitives.WalkEncoding`, `flagquantum.algorithms.primitives.SpectralBlockEncoding`, `flagquantum.algorithms.primitives.spectral_block_encoding`, `flagquantum.algorithms.primitives.subnormalisation`
- **Runtime modes:** `local_statevector`
- **Hardware:** `cpu`
- **Gradient support:** `not_applicable`
- **Distribution semantics:** `single_device_fast_path`
- **Start:** [quick example](../../examples/algorithms/block_encoding.py)
- **Documentation:** [guide](../../docs/guides/ALGORITHMS.md)
- **Known boundary:** A construction and a protocol, and nothing about cost, accuracy, or scale. The construction is the direct spectral reflection U = [[A, sqrt(I - A**2)], [sqrt(I - A**2), -A]] with A = H / alpha, emitted as one dense matrix on n + 1 qubits and built from torch.linalg.eigh of the input; the cost is therefore diagonalising the caller's matrix, and there is no gate-efficient synthesis, no block encoding of a matrix whose dimension is not a power of two, and no sparsity or structure the construction exploits. No error bound is reported, because the construction is exact for a matrix that is exactly Hermitian and an approximate H is the caller's error rather than one this unit estimates. One implementation ships (the spectral one) and one flag wire is used; two protocols are the surface consumers are written against rather than a built family, and a Pauli linear-combination encoding -- whose preparation is a superposition over coefficients rather than the identity, and which needs a multi-wire flag reflection -- is a second implementation behind the same two protocols and is recorded as its own capability rather than left implicit here. The matrix is detached onto the CPU before anything else happens, so no gradient flows from a circuit back to the entries that produced it: this is a classical precomputation over a matrix the caller has already formed, and the module says so rather than leaving it to be discovered. Two divergences from CUDA-Q are deliberate and stated: the flagged block here is +H / alpha, where CUDA-Q's qubitization folds the sign of H into the walk and carries -H / alpha in the block, and the flag ancilla and the operator's qubits are separate arguments here because FlagQuantum addresses qubits individually rather than through a combined register; neither changes what the walk step computes, and the walk identity is asserted for the block this unit emits. The step is asserted twice: as the equality of spectra that is its content, and as the matrix identity W = Z_ancilla U that is its definition, because a spectrum alone does not pin a step -- this construction's step is Hermitian, so it is its own adjoint and its own encoding is a Hermitian unitary on the same register, and only the composed matrix witnesses that the flip is emitted at all. Eleven refusals are deliberate and each is taken before any gate is appended, so a rejected call leaves an empty circuit rather than a half-built encoding: a non-tensor, a non-square, a non-power-of-two, a non-floating-point, a non-finite, a non-Hermitian matrix, the zero matrix, a factor below the spectral norm, a non-positive factor, a non-real factor, and a register that repeats a qubit, has the wrong width, or contains the flag wire. The eigendecomposition is dense double precision and the tolerance of 1e-6 covers the digits sqrt(1 - w**2) loses as w approaches one; a complex Hermitian matrix is accepted and its reflection is complex. The walk step is exposed as two protocol methods rather than as a public walk object holding a moment count, and QSVT and double factorization -- the algorithms the walk step exists to serve -- are absent. This is a circuit constructor: it appends to an ordinary Circuit and adds no target, runtime option, result field, or execution mode, makes no performance, capacity, convergence, or hardware claim, and no Stable Core export follows from this entry.

### Chemistry ansatz construction over the fermionic algebra

Turn an electron count and a spin-orbital count into the UCCSD excitation list, the fermionic generator each excitation evolves, the exact circuit that realizes that exponential, and the Trotterized product that is UCCSD, beside a hardware-efficient and a coupler variant.

- **Maturity:** Development evidence
- **Public API:** `flagquantum.algorithms.chemistry`, `flagquantum.algorithms.chemistry.uccsd_excitations`, `flagquantum.algorithms.chemistry.UCCSDExcitations`, `flagquantum.algorithms.chemistry.excitation_operator`, `flagquantum.algorithms.chemistry.single_excitation`, `flagquantum.algorithms.chemistry.double_excitation`, `flagquantum.algorithms.chemistry.uccsd_ansatz`, `flagquantum.algorithms.chemistry.uccsd_factors`, `flagquantum.algorithms.chemistry.coupler_hardware_efficient_ansatz`, `flagquantum.algorithms.chemistry.coupler_hardware_efficient_parameter_count`
- **Runtime modes:** `local_statevector`
- **Hardware:** `cpu`
- **Gradient support:** `exact`
- **Distribution semantics:** `single_device_fast_path`
- **Start:** [quick-start guide](../../flagquantum/algorithms/README.md)
- **Documentation:** [guide](../../flagquantum/algorithms/README.md)
- **Known boundary:** The ansatz half of a chemistry workload and not the driver half. This module does not read molecular integrals, solve a Hartree-Fock problem, choose a basis set, select an active space, or taper a symmetry; a caller supplies the integrals or the Hamiltonian and this unit supplies the state-preparation circuits, and the sibling entry algorithms_chemistry_hamiltonian is the unit that produces that Hamiltonian from a geometry. There is no PySCF or other driver dependency here, so this unit still names no molecular geometry, no orbital ordering convention, and no energy reference. The gate sequences are the ones CUDA-Q's singleExcitation, doubleExcitation, uccsd, and hwe kernels apply, so a workload built around them transfers, and two divergences are deliberate and stated rather than silent. Every excitation requires its occupied indices to sit below its virtual ones, which is the only arrangement the UCCSD excitation lists produce; CUDA-Q accepts the opposite order and emits a circuit whose conditional ladders are empty, so it becomes a local rotation rather than an excitation, and this unit refuses that input instead. And uccsd_excitations fills the lowest n_electrons spin orbitals for every electron count, while CUDA-Q's get_uccsd_excitations divides by two before rounding up, so the two agree when the electron count is even and CUDA-Q places an occupied index above a virtual one when it is odd. Angles are Python numbers or scalar tensors that keep their graph, so a caller screens gradients, but no gradient is computed here and no optimizer is supplied. The Trotterized product is the algorithms package's own product formula, so the composition order and the two orders available are that unit's, no error bound is reported, and a product formula is exact only when the generators commute. Nothing here executes a circuit, allocates a device buffer, or reads a runtime option; the runtime mode named is the path the returned Circuit is run on.

### Hartree-Fock and the molecular qubit Hamiltonian

Turn a molecular geometry into the second-quantized Hamiltonian a variational chemistry workload consumes: a restricted closed-shell Hartree-Fock solve, the atomic-to-molecular-orbital transform, an optional frozen core and active space, a Jordan-Wigner observable, and an exact S_z = 0 energy where the determinant space allows it.

- **Maturity:** Development evidence
- **Public API:** `flagquantum.algorithms.molecular`, `flagquantum.algorithms.molecular.create_molecular_hamiltonian`, `flagquantum.algorithms.molecular.restricted_hartree_fock`, `flagquantum.algorithms.molecular.molecular_orbital_integrals`, `flagquantum.algorithms.molecular.second_quantized_hamiltonian`, `flagquantum.algorithms.molecular.fold_frozen_core`, `flagquantum.algorithms.molecular.exact_ground_state_energy`, `flagquantum.algorithms.molecular.MolecularHamiltonian`, `flagquantum.algorithms.molecular.HartreeFockSolution`, `flagquantum.algorithms.molecular.MolecularHamiltonian.energy_offset`
- **Runtime modes:** `local_statevector`
- **Hardware:** `cpu`
- **Gradient support:** `not_applicable`
- **Distribution semantics:** `single_device_fast_path`
- **Start:** [quick-start guide](../../flagquantum/algorithms/README.md)
- **Documentation:** [guide](../../flagquantum/algorithms/README.md)
- **Known boundary:** The driver covers what the integral engine underneath it covers, so the basis is STO-3G and the elements are H through Ne; there is no basis-set selection, no correlation-consistent basis, and no comparison with a value from one, because that would compare two different Hamiltonians. The Hartree-Fock solve is restricted and closed-shell, so it is a statement about a singlet and nothing else: a multiplicity other than one is refused rather than approximated by a solution of the wrong symmetry, and so is an odd electron count. It starts from the bare core Hamiltonian and converges to the fixed point nearest it, so the energy is stationary in the density and is not proven to be the lowest one; a second solution at a different energy is not searched for. It is undamped and needs a few tens of iterations at the standard geometries, and it fails closed with the largest density change it reached rather than returning an unconverged density. There is no point-group symmetry, no symmetry-adapted orbital, and no tapering of the qubit Hamiltonian, and no spin-orbit or relativistic correction. The frozen core is exact for the active space it defines and not an approximation of the full problem: the frozen orbitals are held doubly occupied, so the energy it gives is the energy of that reduced Hamiltonian. The exact energy is dense diagonalisation of the S_z = 0 sector, so it is available only at or below 1024 determinants and is None above that; a larger molecule still gets a Hamiltonian, an SCF energy, and a Hartree-Fock determinant, and what it does not get is an energy to compare an eigensolver against. That value is the active-space energy, where CUDA-Q's fci_energy comes from PySCF over the full space, so the two agree only when no orbital is frozen and no active space is chosen. Nothing here executes a circuit: the Hamiltonian is returned as an Observable and the energy is a number, and it is the caller who runs a variational ansatz on it.

### Molecular Gaussian integrals in the STO-3G basis

Evaluate the overlap, kinetic, nuclear-attraction, and two-electron integrals of a molecule in this repository, over contracted Cartesian Gaussians, with no chemistry package behind the evaluation.

- **Maturity:** Development evidence
- **Public API:** `flagquantum.algorithms.chemistry_integrals`, `flagquantum.algorithms.chemistry_integrals.molecular_integrals`, `flagquantum.algorithms.chemistry_integrals.MolecularIntegrals`, `flagquantum.algorithms.chemistry_integrals.MolecularGeometry`, `flagquantum.algorithms.chemistry_integrals.AtomicOrbital`, `flagquantum.algorithms.chemistry_integrals.MolecularGeometry.from_angstrom`
- **Runtime modes:** `not_applicable`
- **Hardware:** `cpu`
- **Gradient support:** `not_applicable`
- **Distribution semantics:** `single_device_fast_path`
- **Start:** [quick-start guide](../../flagquantum/algorithms/README.md)
- **Documentation:** [guide](../../flagquantum/algorithms/README.md)
- **Known boundary:** A numerical kernel, not a chemistry package. The basis table is STO-3G for H through Ne and nothing else: an element outside it is refused rather than given a contraction this repository does not hold, and a basis name other than sto-3g is refused rather than silently substituted. Even inside that table the published STO-3G contraction coefficients are rounded to eight decimals, so the overlap is unit only to about 1e-8 rather than exactly, and every other integral carries that same rounding. The evaluation is dense and direct: there is no integral screening, no point-group or permutation symmetry beyond the eight-fold index symmetry of the electron-repulsion tensor, and no use of the sparsity a real molecule has, so the cost is the fourth power of the number of basis functions with a large constant. No analytic nuclear gradient is produced, so no geometry optimisation and no force constant is available, and no integral derivative with respect to any other parameter exists here. The engine is Cartesian rather than spherical, so a d shell would carry six functions rather than five; the STO-3G table reaches only s and p, so that distinction is not observable at this level and would have to be settled before a larger basis is added. Torch is the only dependency, which is why the tensors are torch tensors and not arrays; numpy is not used. Nothing here selects a molecule, a charge, a multiplicity, or an active space, and no Hartree-Fock problem is solved.

### Clifford data regression over near-Clifford training circuits

Fit the affine relation between what a circuit should give and what the noise makes it give, on Clifford training circuits built by snapping each rotation of the target to its nearest quarter turn, then apply that relation to the target's own noisy expectation.

- **Maturity:** Development evidence
- **Public API:** `flagquantum.algorithms.cdr`, `flagquantum.algorithms.run_cdr`, `flagquantum.algorithms.clifford_variants`
- **Runtime modes:** `density_matrix`
- **Hardware:** `cpu`
- **Gradient support:** `unsupported`
- **Distribution semantics:** `single_device_fast_path`
- **Start:** [quick example](../../examples/algorithms/cdr.py)
- **Documentation:** [guide](../../docs/guides/ALGORITHMS.md)
- **Known boundary:** Clifford data regression whose training set is a rewrite, not a device run, and whose premise is the affine relation and only that relation. The ideal values are read from exact density simulation of each training circuit rather than from the stabilizer representation, so Clifford membership is enforced -- every training circuit is accepted by require_clifford_program before it is measured -- but never exploited, and no simulation-cost, capacity or scaling claim follows from the fact that the training circuits are Clifford. Nothing here is cheaper than the target it corrects. The method's one assumption is that the ideal expectation is affine in the noisy one over the region the training circuits span; nothing checks it and it is not checkable from the measurements alone. The diagnostic that exposes a violated premise is the largest absolute residual the fit left, and a two-point fit's residual is reported as absent rather than as an arithmetic zero, because two points determine a line and the zero would be an arithmetic identity presented as a check. The estimate carries no error bound and no confidence interval, and no improvement over the unmitigated value is guaranteed: the residual is the only warning. Only the five single-wire theta rotations are snappable -- phase, rx, ry, rz and u1 -- and each is rewritten exactly on the quarter-turn grid, to 1.8e-16 over all twenty entries in complex128, up to a single global phase that leaves the program's density matrix unchanged; a program containing no such rotation is refused by name rather than trained on itself, and cphase, crx, cry, crz, rxx, ryy, rzz, u2 and u3 have no named-Clifford word at all, so a program built from them is refused by the stabilizer engine rather than snapped approximately. A rotation whose angle is an unbound parameter has no nearest turn and is refused. Residue zero rewrites to the empty word, so a rotation already on the grid is dropped rather than emitted as an identity. A noise model keys its channels on gate names, so a model that names an operation the rewrite removes is refused, because every training point would then be measured under less noise than the target and the line would fit a difference the rewrite created; a caller who needs such a model supplies the training circuits explicitly and owns that claim. A model that declares a readout rule is refused for the same reason the extrapolation and cancellation units refuse one: the estimate is Tr(O rho) and classical readout confusion is applied after measurement. Readout-error mitigation is a separate unit beside this one, flagquantum.algorithms.plan_readout_mitigation, which corrects a measured distribution rather than fitting a value that was never read out through a confusion. A program that declares a measurement or observable request is refused too, because this unit reads Tr(O rho) from the state and consumes no request, and returning an estimate while a declared request stayed unused would report a measurement that never happened. The training set is capped at 64 circuits, which is 128 exact simulations, and a caller-supplied set is capped at the same number. A training set whose noisy values do not separate is refused rather than fitted on rounding, at a declared floor of 1e-9 of the span rather than at a value reached by tuning. Measured: for h(0); ry(0, 0.7); ry(1, 0.4); cx(0, 1) observed as Z0 + X1, with two-qubit depolarizing noise at 0.06 on the cx and one-qubit depolarizing noise at 0.03 on the h, the three training points read ideal 0.0, -1.0, 1.0 against noisy 0.0, -0.898560, 0.936000, the fit reads slope 1.090028330 and intercept -1.360e-02 with a residual of 1.360355e-02 over one degree of freedom, and the target's exact value -0.254799345 is read as -0.214372678 unmitigated and -0.247275845 mitigated, so the correction closes 4.043e-02 of the 4.043e-02 gap to 7.524e-03 and the residual is what says the closure is incomplete; on the same target with only the cx noise, the same three training circuits fall on a line to 1.110e-16 and the mitigated value is on the exact one to 1.665e-16, so the residual separates a premise that held from one that did not rather than being decorative. On h(0); ry(0, 0.6); cx(0, 1) observed as Z0 with two-qubit depolarizing noise at 0.06 on the cx and one-qubit depolarizing noise at 0.03 on the h, the two training points read ideal 0.0 and -1.0 against noisy -5.6e-17 and -0.936000001, so the fitted slope is the reciprocal of the shrinkage the model applied, 1.068376067, and the target's exact -0.564642473 is read as -0.528505356 unmitigated and recovered to 4.4e-16 mitigated -- a two-point fit, so that agreement is an arithmetic identity and the residual is reported as absent rather than as evidence. Demonstration scale: two-wire circuits on the CPU density-matrix path with at most three training points in these measurements. No performance, scaling, hardware or fault-tolerance claim follows from it, and no advantage over sampling the target directly is claimed.

### COBYLA trust-region constrained search

Minimize a scalar objective under inequality constraints by minimizing, at every iteration, a linear program over a box that prices the largest violated constraint, with a radius and a price the run adjusts on the step's own evidence.

- **Maturity:** Development evidence
- **Public API:** `flagquantum.algorithms.cobyla`
- **Runtime modes:** `not_applicable`
- **Hardware:** `cpu`
- **Gradient support:** `not_applicable`
- **Distribution semantics:** `not_applicable`
- **Start:** [quick example](../../examples/algorithms/cobyla_optimizer.py)
- **Documentation:** [guide](../../docs/guides/ALGORITHMS.md)
- **Known boundary:** The step is the exact minimizer of a convex subproblem, and the subproblem is a linearization of a problem that is not convex, so converged reports that the trust region reached its floor and not that the point is a constrained optimum. converged is exactly rho <= rhoend, it says nothing about the objective's slope and nothing about feasibility, and the same run can report converged=True with feasible=False: a constraint no point satisfies is a case this unit answers with a point rather than an exception. The price on a violated constraint is a schedule and not a calibrated weight, and that is the sharpest edge here. It starts at penalty and doubles when an accepted step fails to reduce a violation the models predicted it would remove or when the true violation came in worse than the linearization predicted, and it stops at penalty_ceiling; no Lagrange-multiplier estimate is formed and no exact penalty is computed. The consequence is measured rather than described: on one quadratic whose unconstrained minimum is (3, 3) under x + y <= 4, penalty=1.0 returns [2.5, 2.5] with value 0.5 and a residual of 1.0 while penalty=2.0 returns the feasible optimum [2.0, 2.0] with value 2.0, so the cheaper price returns a point that violates the constraint and scores better as an objective, and feasible and residual are reported beside value because a caller who reads value alone reads that point as the better answer. No equality constraint, no bounds syntax, no restart, no multi-start, no basin hopping and no simulated annealing exist, so a constrained problem with several feasible regions is only reachable from the region the starting point is in. Every constraint is a callable that receives a flat view of the parameters and must return one finite scalar, and a constraint is satisfied when that value is at least zero, so an equality constraint has to arrive as two inequalities written by the caller. The premise is a deterministic objective and locally affine models: the objective and every constraint are modelled by finite differences at a step of the current radius, so an objective whose value moves between two evaluations of the same point turns each model into noise, and the radius is the only statement the run makes about the scale at which the models are meaningful. The constraints are linearized and not evaluated as a feasible set, so a run can accept a step whose true violation is larger than the models agreed on, which is one of the two conditions that raise the price rather than one the unit prevents. The cost is a count of objective and constraint calls together and not a latency, a device cost or a parallel schedule: each iteration builds a model at the current point and one at the trial point, a model costs one base evaluation plus one per parameter, every constraint is evaluated at every point the objective is, the trial point of an iteration is usually a point the previous model already read and those calls are served from a per-run cache rather than repeated, and nothing is vectorized or batched. The equalities and inequalities reported here are the numerical values the caller's callables returned and nothing is certified about them. The optimizer executes nothing itself: it calls callables the caller supplies and never inspects them, so no runtime mode is selected, no circuit is built and no observable is measured. Both schedules, the iteration budget, the three tolerances and the price ceiling are the caller's, no checkpoint or resume protocol exists, and the one arithmetic deviation the unit does make is that a starting point that is not a finite floating tensor of at least one value is refused rather than promoted. It adds no root-level fq name and leaves the Stable Core surface untouched.

### Classical data encoding

Encode a classical feature vector into a quantum state, either as the amplitudes of a prepared state or as one rotation per wire.

- **Maturity:** Development evidence
- **Public API:** `flagquantum.algorithms.data_encoding`, `flagquantum.algorithms.data_encoding.amplitude_encode`, `flagquantum.algorithms.data_encoding.angular_encode`, `flagquantum.algorithms.data_encoding.append_angular_encode`
- **Runtime modes:** `local_statevector`
- **Hardware:** `cpu`
- **Gradient support:** `exact`
- **Distribution semantics:** `single_device_fast_path`
- **Start:** [quick-start guide](../../docs/guides/ALGORITHMS.md)
- **Documentation:** [guide](../../docs/guides/ALGORITHMS.md)
- **Known boundary:** Both halves return or extend a circuit and neither executes it, selects a runtime, or carries a simulator object. The amplitude half spends the whole state vector on the data, so its classical input is already 2**n amplitudes and the preparation is delegated to the state-preparation primitive rather than made cheaper; it is exact only to the working precision of that primitive's solve, up to one free global phase, and it is not a State object with a pad-and-renormalise contract on an existing state. The angular half carries only one angle per wire, so it packs far less data per wire than amplitude encoding does; it is a feature map, not a compression, and no advantage, performance, or hardware claim follows from either half. Neither encoding is selected by the default runtime, and neither is reachable from kernel-language code.

### Gate and circuit folding by an exact identity

Lengthen a program by inserting each instruction's own inverse and then the instruction again, either distributed one instruction at a time or as whole repeated bodies, and report the length ratio the fold actually realized, the instructions it added, and the folded program, and, against a noise model, how many channel applications the fold added on each wire.

- **Maturity:** Development evidence
- **Public API:** `flagquantum.algorithms.folding`, `flagquantum.algorithms.fold_program`, `flagquantum.algorithms.FoldingPlan`, `flagquantum.algorithms.FOLDING_STRATEGIES`, `flagquantum.algorithms.FOLDING_PLACEMENTS`, `flagquantum.algorithms.fold_noise_amplification`, `flagquantum.algorithms.FoldNoiseAmplification`, `flagquantum.algorithms.FOLD_NOISE_SCHEMA`
- **Runtime modes:** `local_statevector`, `density_matrix`
- **Hardware:** `cpu`
- **Gradient support:** `not_applicable`
- **Distribution semantics:** `single_device_fast_path`
- **Start:** [quick example](../../examples/algorithms/folding.py)
- **Documentation:** [guide](../../docs/guides/ALGORITHMS.md)
- **Known boundary:** Gate and circuit folding, whose premise is about the device and whose output is a length ratio rather than a noise estimate. The fold is U (U-dagger U)^m, so it is an exact identity for every unitary the body is made of and the only deviation it contributes is the dtype's own rounding: measured on the five-instruction body h, cx, rz, ry, t the amplitude gap at complex128 is 1.6e-16 for both strategies, which is the floor of the state comparison rather than a number the fold added. That is what makes the method different from the three units beside it -- nothing is approximated and nothing is fitted -- and it is also what makes a channel in the body a refusal rather than something to skip: a noise channel has no unitary inverse, a fold that dropped it would change the ideal signal rather than only the noise, and the refusal names the instruction and the reason. The assumption the method does rest on is not visible in the program and is not checked by it: repeating an instruction has to repeat that instruction's error and change no other instruction's error, so crosstalk, drift between the unfolded and folded points, and a calibration that moved between them all violate the premise while leaving the program looking correct. What the plan returns is therefore a ratio and not a strength: the realized scale factor is a length ratio, the conversion from a length ratio to a noise strength belongs to the extrapolation the caller runs, and a caller who reads the ratio as a measured error strength has substituted the device's behaviour for the model's. The strength is a separate reading rather than an implication of the ratio. flagquantum.algorithms.fold_noise_amplification counts channel applications -- one channel the model applies at one instruction -- on the body and on the folded program and reports the ratio per qubit, which needs no arithmetic on a channel parameter and so holds for a depolarizing channel, a damping channel and a mixture alike, and it is exact rather than first order. Measured on the five-instruction body h, cx, rz, ry, t with depolarizing at 0.03 on every gate, a gate fold to a realized ratio of 1.4 takes six applications to eight, which is 1.3333, and it is uneven: qubit 0 goes from four to six while qubit 1 stays at two, because the pair the gate strategy spends is two instructions on one qubit. A circuit fold does not restore the ratio either: repeating the whole body to a realized ratio of 3.0 takes the same six applications to seventeen, which is 2.8333, because the adjoint it inserts carries tdg, a name this model does not list, so qubit 1 reaches 3.0 exactly and the qubit holding the t reaches 2.75. The reading therefore separates three causes that no plan states -- a model that names part of the body, an inverse whose own name the model does not carry, and a strategy that spends its pairs on a subset of the qubits -- and a qubit the model never touches is reported as a qubit with no ratio rather than as a qubit whose ratio is one. The realized factor is the abscissa flagquantum.algorithms.run_zne fits against, not the requested one, because it is the ratio the noise saw; the plain scaling of that unit multiplies a channel parameter and is a different unit of scale, so a call naming both a fold and a scaling callable is refused rather than composed. The two strategies are not interchangeable. A circuit fold repeats whole bodies, so its realized ratio is 1 + 2m and a request between two odd integers overshoots: on the five-instruction body a request of 1.4 is met by 3.0 in fifteen instructions. A gate fold distributes the same pairs one instruction at a time and reaches a request exactly whenever the arithmetic allows: the same request of 1.4 is met exactly in seven instructions, and a request of 2.2 is met exactly in eleven where the circuit strategy's next reachable ratio, 3.0, costs it fifteen, so the exact factor is also the cheaper one. Because both strategies reach a ratio in steps of two instructions, distinct requests can realize one length: under a circuit fold of the five-instruction body the requests 1.4 and 2.6 both realize 3.0, and a run handed such a set is refused by naming the duplicated ratio rather than by failing inside the linear solve, because repeated abscissas make that system singular. A gate fold under the default placement spends its pairs leftmost first, so its added gates land where the strategy puts them rather than where the caller's observable is sensitive, and a readout blind to a pair reads a flat curve over a program whose noise genuinely grew: on the five-instruction body with depolarizing at 0.03 the added pair lands on the leading h, and a zz(0, 1) readout reads 0.672956129888 at both ratio 1.0 and ratio 1.4 while xx(0, 1) reads 0.242863810368 and 0.223823288052. Four further refusals are deliberate and each names what it measured: a scale factor below one, which would have to remove instructions and change the ideal signal; a non-finite factor; an empty program, which has no instruction to fold; and a custom instruction whose matrix is not unitary or not square, because the fold repeats it with its own conjugate transpose and the identity would not hold. A program carrying a noise channel is refused before any of that, and the fold preserves the body's measurement and observable nodes as well as its instructions, so a fold of a program that declares them keeps them. This is a program transform, not an execution path: it builds a flagquantum.core.ir.CircuitIR and the existing runtime executes it, so no new target, runtime option, result field or execution mode is added, and no Stable Core export follows. Where a gate fold's pairs go is a second choice beside which strategy to use, and it is the one question here whose answer is in the noise model. flagquantum.algorithms.FOLDING_PLACEMENTS names two rules. body_order is the default and spends the pairs on the body's leading instructions, one instruction at a time, and needs no model. model_weighted spends them on the instructions the model declares the most channel applications at, heaviest first with ties broken by body order, and an instruction the model names nowhere receives none. Both rules are one cycle over a different sequence, so they realize one scale factor and insert one pair count -- the count is fixed by the request and the body's length -- and differ in the program, which is why a plan and a reading both carry the placement and two placements of one request carry two identities. What the choice buys is reach, and the case that shows it is the one where the default rule cannot reach the noise at all: on the five-instruction body with a model naming only t, a request of 1.4 is one pair, the default rule spends it on the leading h and the curve is one number at every abscissa (0.297856195029 measured at all four points, the spread being the last bit of two contractions), so the extrapolation returns the unmitigated measurement unchanged having paid two instructions for no added noise, while the placed rule spends the same pair on t, takes one application to two, and moves the curve (0.297856195, 0.285941947, 0.274504270, 0.263524099). What it does not buy is accuracy: at that request the placed estimate is 1.9408e-02 from the noiseless value where the flat curve's is 1.2411e-02, because one pair on a lone noisy instruction doubles that instruction's noise while the abscissa moves by 1.4, so the curve is far from linear in the length ratio it is fitted against. A placement is a statement about which instructions a fold reaches and never a claim that the estimate is the better one. Two limits are deliberate. A circuit fold repeats the whole body and so has no placement to choose: it is refused rather than reported with a rule it did not apply, and run_zne refuses a placement when it scales the model instead of folding. The placed rule reads only how many channel applications a model declares at an instruction and never what they are worth, so two models declaring one channel each of very different strength at different instructions are placed identically: nothing here states a per-instruction magnitude in a unit comparable across channel families, and choosing a placement by measured amplification rather than by a declared count would need a simulate-then-place loop, which this unit does not run because it is a program transform and never simulates. Not here: any fold of a noisy program, any strategy beyond the two named, and any placement of a circuit fold's pairs or any placement chosen by a strength rather than by a count. Demonstration scale: two- to five-instruction circuits on the CPU statevector and density-matrix paths, with folds up to a ratio of seven. No performance, scaling, hardware or fault-tolerance claim follows from it.

### Block encoding of a Hamiltonian given as a weighted sum of Pauli words

Encode a Hamiltonian written as a sum of weighted Pauli words into the flagged block of a unitary at the sum of the coefficient magnitudes, preparing the coefficients in an index register and selecting each word on it, and append one qubitization walk step whose eigenphases carry the encoded spectrum.

- **Maturity:** Development evidence
- **Public API:** `flagquantum.algorithms.primitives.linear_combination`, `flagquantum.algorithms.primitives.LinearCombinationEncoding`
- **Runtime modes:** `local_statevector`
- **Hardware:** `cpu`
- **Gradient support:** `not_applicable`
- **Distribution semantics:** `single_device_fast_path`
- **Start:** [quick example](../../examples/algorithms/linear_combination.py)
- **Documentation:** [guide](../../docs/guides/ALGORITHMS.md)
- **Known boundary:** A construction and a protocol, and nothing about cost, accuracy, or scale. The subnormalisation is the sum of the coefficient magnitudes, stated from the caller's data before any circuit exists, and no matrix is formed to measure a norm: it is at least the spectral norm, so every eigenvalue of the encoded block lies in [-1, 1], but it is deliberately not the Frobenius norm the spectral construction uses and the two are not even ordered -- the Frobenius norm of a Pauli product on n wires is 2 ** (n / 2), so a one-wire sum can have a Frobenius norm above the sum of its magnitudes. The preparation is a dense classical precomputation over 2 ** k amplitudes, exponential in the index-ancilla count k = max(1, ceil(log2 m)), and the select is one multi-controlled Pauli per term, so this is a demonstration-scale encoding and not a gate-efficient synthesis: there is no amplitude amplification, no uniform-preparation trick, and no gate-count claim. The ancilla count is k + max(k - 2, 0) and not one wire per term. The walk identity is subspace-restricted: the construction is exactly block diagonal in the ladder register, but off the ladder-zero subspace it is not the identity there, so the arccosine spectrum with multiplicity two is asserted on the ladder-zero subspace, and the multiplicity is per eigenvector rather than per distinct eigenvalue -- a degenerate sum multiplies it, measured at 8 and 4 on two all-identity sums. The flagged block is read back at the emitted circuit's gate precision, so the measured residual against the exact sum is of order 1e-08 rather than machine epsilon, and that is precision and not approximation in the construction. A complex coefficient is refused by name rather than reduced to its real part, because the preparation primitive carries no relative phase and the sum would not be the operator the caller named. A coefficient that requires a gradient is read as the number it holds, so no gradient flows from the circuit back to the coefficients. No alpha parameter exists, because the factor is determined by the data and a caller who wants a larger subnormalisation scales the Hamiltonian. An all-identity term is carried here, where the Trotter module must refuse one, because the term only contributes to the factor and to the identity block. Two divergences from CUDA-Q are inherited from the protocol rather than chosen here: the flagged block is +H / alpha where CUDA-Q's qubitization folds the sign of H into the walk, and the flag register and the operator's wires are separate arguments because FlagQuantum addresses qubits individually. No Stable Core export follows from this entry.

### Logical-layer resource estimation over a Clifford+T program

Read a program's Clifford and T tallies and its schedule depth out of the compiler's own static estimate, and cost them on a rotated surface code at a distance the caller names: logical layers, physical qubits, surface-code cycles, and their product.

- **Maturity:** Development evidence
- **Public API:** `flagquantum.algorithms.logical_resources`, `flagquantum.algorithms.logical_resources.estimate_logical_resources`, `flagquantum.algorithms.logical_resources.surface_code_qubits_per_logical`, `flagquantum.algorithms.logical_resources.LogicalResourceReport`, `flagquantum.algorithms.logical_resources.LOGICAL_RESOURCE_BASIS`, `flagquantum.algorithms.logical_resources.SURFACE_CODE_MODEL`
- **Runtime modes:** `not_applicable`
- **Hardware:** `cpu`
- **Gradient support:** `not_applicable`
- **Distribution semantics:** `not_applicable`
- **Start:** [quick example](../../examples/algorithms/logical_resources.py)
- **Documentation:** [guide](../../docs/guides/ALGORITHMS.md)
- **Known boundary:** A count over a program's operation sequence and a footprint over a stated code model, and nothing measured: no wall-clock time, no resident memory, no allocation, and no fidelity. No physical error rate is read and no logical error rate is reported, because a threshold fit's prefactor and threshold are a device's numbers rather than this unit's, so this report says how much hardware a logical program would occupy and for how long, and never how often it would fail. The input has to already be Clifford+T, and two families are refused by name rather than counted: a parametric rotation is refused rather than synthesised, because this unit reads opcodes and runs no legalization, so a T-count taken over a rotation the program still contains would be the count of a circuit nobody will run; angle synthesis for an arbitrary angle is still absent here, and a compound operation whose T-count is its decomposition's -- a Toffoli, a controlled swap -- is refused because a Toffoli is not a Clifford gate and the decompositions do not agree on what it costs. No distillation factory, magic-state budget, or routing overhead is included, so the physical figure is a floor for a circuit of these layers rather than a compiled estimate. No placement, scheduling, or layout pass runs and no device is modelled, so the physical qubit count is a patch count: a device's pitch, connectivity, yield, and component choices are absent, and so is every code family other than the rotated surface code named on the report. The gate-level tally is not re-derived: operation counts, the T family, and the schedule depth are flagquantum.compiler.resource_estimation.estimate_resources, passed through rather than recomputed, and the tests assert that agreement against the compiler's own private T-family table so the two cannot drift apart silently. One code model ships, rotated_surface_code_2d, with 2 d^2 - 1 physical qubits per patch and d surface-code cycles per logical layer; the refusal of a data-dependent program is the compiler's own, taken before any figure is computed. The unit executes nothing and adds no target, runtime option, result field, or execution mode, so no runtime or distributed semantics follow from this entry, and no Stable Core export follows either.

### Nelder-Mead simplex search

Minimize a scalar objective with no derivative and no random draw, by reflecting, expanding, contracting and shrinking a simplex of n + 1 vertices until two measured spreads fall under the caller's thresholds.

- **Maturity:** Development evidence
- **Public API:** `flagquantum.algorithms.nelder_mead`
- **Runtime modes:** `not_applicable`
- **Hardware:** `cpu`
- **Gradient support:** `not_applicable`
- **Distribution semantics:** `not_applicable`
- **Start:** [quick example](../../examples/algorithms/nelder_mead_optimizer.py)
- **Documentation:** [guide](../../docs/guides/ALGORITHMS.md)
- **Known boundary:** This is a local method and its converged flag is not a statement about a minimum. A run stops when the objective values across the simplex fall under atol and the vertex positions across the simplex fall under tolerance, and a simplex can collapse onto a point that is neither a local nor a global minimum; the widely used variant this unit implements has no complete convergence theory in more than one dimension, which the returned result carries in its own limitations rather than leaving to this field. The failure is measured and not merely described: on the two-well quartic (x**2 - 1)**2 + 0.1x started at 0.5 the run reports converged=True on a value 0.199984362162 above the value it reaches from -0.5, and tightening the thresholds does not repair it because the simplex is inside one basin. There is no restart, no multi-start, no basin hopping and no simulated annealing, so the basin the starting simplex is placed in is the only one a run can reach, which is why initial_simplex is public and why the (n + 1, n) simplex is the caller's to build. The premise is a deterministic objective: every decision the method makes is a comparison of two objective values, so an objective whose value moves between two evaluations of the same point turns each decision into noise and the trajectory into a walk, and a stochastic objective is the subject of the spsa unit beside this one rather than something this unit tolerates. A starting simplex that does not span all n parameter directions is refused by name, which is deliberate and is also a boundary: the collinear construction the published counterexample uses cannot be expressed through this entry point, so the counterexample is refused rather than reproduced, and what remains reachable is a local minimum. The cost is a count of objective evaluations and not a latency, a device cost or a parallel schedule: vertices are evaluated one at a time, one evaluation per call, with no vectorization of the simplex, and the count is bracketed by (n + 1) + iterations at the lower end and (n + 1) + (n + 1) * iterations at the upper end rather than being a function of the parameter count alone. No derivative, curvature or uncertainty is estimated or reported, because the method reads only the order of objective values and two objectives with the same ordering produce the same trajectory. Bounds, constraint handling, feasibility and penalty notions are absent, so a constrained problem is only as well posed as the caller's own transformation of it. The optimizer executes nothing itself: it calls a callable the caller supplies and never inspects it, so no runtime mode is selected, no circuit is built and no observable is measured. The four coefficients, the initial step, both thresholds and the iteration budget are the caller's, no checkpoint or resume protocol exists, and the one arithmetic deviation the unit does make is that a simplex not carrying the parameters' own dtype is refused rather than silently promoted. It adds no root-level fq name and leaves the Stable Core surface untouched.

### Probabilistic error cancellation over a declared Pauli channel

Invert a noise channel the model declares exactly, by decomposing its inverse into a signed combination of Pauli words read off the channel's Pauli transfer matrix, and report the mitigated estimate beside the sampling overhead that combination implies.

- **Maturity:** Development evidence
- **Public API:** `flagquantum.algorithms.pec`, `flagquantum.algorithms.run_pec`, `flagquantum.algorithms.pauli_twirl_decomposition`
- **Runtime modes:** `density_matrix`
- **Hardware:** `cpu`
- **Gradient support:** `unsupported`
- **Distribution semantics:** `single_device_fast_path`
- **Start:** [quick example](../../examples/algorithms/pec.py)
- **Documentation:** [guide](../../docs/guides/ALGORITHMS.md)
- **Known boundary:** Probabilistic error cancellation whose premise is the declared noise model and only that model. The unit inverts the channel the model declares, at the location the model declares it: an error the model does not carry -- a miscalibrated gate, leakage, drift between the declaration and the run -- survives the inversion untouched, so a mitigated value close to the ideal is a statement about the model before it is a statement about a device. The decomposition is exact only for a channel whose Pauli transfer matrix is diagonal, which is what makes it a Pauli channel, and only while no transfer eigenvalue reaches zero, which is what makes the inverse exist at all. Both boundaries are checked at 1e-6 and refused by name rather than approximated, each refusal quoting the magnitude it measured: bit_flip, phase_flip, depolarizing, two_qubit_depolarizing and phase_damping are admitted at the probabilities tested, while amplitude_damping (1.000e-01 off-diagonal), coherent_overrotation (1.987e-01), reset_error (5.000e-02) and thermal_relaxation (1.000e+00) are refused, and bit_flip at one half is refused for a vanishing eigenvalue at Y rather than for being outside the family. The tolerance sits two orders above the complex64 floor a single-precision channel is measured at and five below the smallest refusal recorded here. The estimate is a quasi-probability average: it is not a physical density matrix and it can be non-positive, which is why the unit returns a value rather than a state. It carries no confidence interval, and sampling_overhead is gamma squared -- the shot cost a sampled implementation of the same combination would pay, because every signed weight carries its sign into the variance -- rather than a measurement of this path, which evaluates each term exactly and consumes no shot. gamma is at least one, equals one only for a channel that is itself a Pauli conjugation, and composes multiplicatively over locations, so the price of a model is the product of the prices of its sites. The cost is bounded: a combination needing more than 1024 exact programs is refused with the count it needed, which is four terms per single-qubit noise location and sixteen per two-qubit one. A model that declares a readout rule is refused rather than measured, because the observable is read as Tr(O rho) before measurement and classical readout confusion is not part of rho; mitigating readout is a separate unit beside this one, flagquantum.algorithms.plan_readout_mitigation, which inverts a declared confusion on a measured vector rather than a channel on a state. A circuit that declares its own input state is refused, because the unit re-executes the program from its IR once per term and a program built from an IR begins at the all-zero state. The circuit's batch size must be one, since one expectation is read per term. A noise model that puts no channel in the program is refused, because an identity model's mitigation is the unmitigated value under another name. Measured on h(0); cx(0, 1) with the observable zz(0, 1) and bit_flip at 0.1 on the cx: the two-location composite reads 0.639999995231628 unmitigated and 0.999999999999999 mitigated in complex128, at gamma 1.562500011642 over 16 terms and 17 exact programs, and the same run at the runtime's default precision reaches 0.999999866503455 from an unmitigated 0.639999806880951, so the floor the method leaves is the precision the channel was declared in. The one-location read of the same program is 0.799999997019768 unmitigated and 1.000000000000000 mitigated at gamma 1.250000004657 over four terms, and the two-location gamma is its square. Demonstration scale: two-wire circuits on the CPU density-matrix path, at most five noise locations. No performance, scaling, hardware or fault-tolerance claim follows from it, and no variance reduction is claimed.

### Readout-error mitigation over a declared classical confusion

Correct a measured distribution for the classical misassignment a NoiseModel's readout rules declare, by grouping those rules into disjoint blocks and applying each block's transpose inverse, with the amplification the correction costs and the negative mass it may carry reported beside the corrected vector.

- **Maturity:** Development evidence
- **Public API:** `flagquantum.algorithms.readout_mitigation`, `flagquantum.algorithms.plan_readout_mitigation`, `flagquantum.algorithms.run_readout_mitigation`, `flagquantum.algorithms.run_readout_mitigation_counts`
- **Runtime modes:** `not_applicable`
- **Hardware:** `cpu`
- **Gradient support:** `not_applicable`
- **Distribution semantics:** `not_applicable`
- **Start:** [quick example](../../examples/algorithms/readout_mitigation.py)
- **Documentation:** [guide](../../docs/guides/ALGORITHMS.md)
- **Known boundary:** Readout-error mitigation over a declared classical confusion, which is a linear inverse rather than a fit: the correction is exact exactly when the declared confusion is the device's confusion, and nothing here checks that. A readout error that is miscalibrated, that drifts between calibration and use, or that is correlated in a way the model does not carry survives the inversion untouched, so a corrected vector that lands on the ideal distribution is a statement about the model before it is a statement about a device. The declared index convention and the runtime's flat index convention differ: a calibration table is written with the true outcome as the row index, while flagquantum.noise.model.NoiseModel.apply_readout_probabilities applies that table transposed to a vector whose qubit 0 is the most significant bit, so the correcting operator is the transpose inverse inv(P.T) and not inv(P). The two agree on a symmetric confusion and differ on every asymmetric one -- by 0.14285714 in the maximum entry on ((0.9, 0.1), (0.2, 0.8)) -- which is why the relation is asserted directly rather than through agreement with a symmetric table. The corrected vector is not a distribution and is not forced to be one: the inverse of a stochastic matrix has negative entries whenever the confusion is invertible and is not a permutation, so negative_mass is the sum of the negative entries, total_variation is the L1 distance to the observed vector, and is_physical states which side of the simplex the result landed on rather than asserting that it must be inside. Nothing here refits, smooths, or projects onto the simplex, and no maximum-likelihood correction is offered. The amplification is the price of the correction and it is the whole map's induced 1-norm: for a map that is the tensor product of disjoint blocks that norm is exactly the product of the blocks' own, not the worst block's, so sampling_overhead composes multiplicatively and equals the worst block's only when there is one block. The standard error bound the result carries is that amplification over the square root of the shot count, which follows because the transpose inverse's infinity norm is the inverse's 1-norm. That bounds the corrected weight's standard error rather than the correction's error against the truth, and the exact per-component variance is the full covariance (diag(d) - d d^T) / N with d the device distribution, not the sum over outcomes of v squared times d times (1 - d) that a per-outcome reading gives: the missing (v^T d)^2 term was measured 40 per cent low on the two-qubit case this unit is tested on, 0.046274639 against 0.064321607. A readout rule naming several qubits is inverted as one block rather than as its marginals, because inverting the marginals inverts a different operator; the correlated table this unit is measured on does not factorize, its joint form read as a matrix having a second singular value of 8.144e-03 where a tensor product leaves rank one. A qubit named by two readout rules is refused rather than composed, because the forward path would apply the confusion twice and the inverse of a doubled confusion is neither correction; an uncovered qubit is left exactly where it was and is named as uncovered rather than given a guessed confusion. A block whose smallest singular value reaches the declared floor is refused rather than inverted, because the inverse's magnitude grows as the reciprocal of that singular value and the correction would report weights no shot count can support; the floor is the caller's and the default SINGULAR_VALUE_FLOOR of 1e-6 is matched to the decomposition tolerance probabilistic error cancellation uses, so a tighter floor refuses more. A correlated block wider than MAX_CORRELATED_BLOCK_QUBITS of 10 qubits is refused before its matrix is built, because inverting it needs a 2048 by 2048 float64 matrix and its singular values. An observed distribution whose rows fall further from one than NORMALIZATION_TOLERANCE of 1e-6 is refused, and one inside that tolerance is corrected rather than rescaled silently; a histogram whose keys are not all the plan's width, whose total is zero, or whose counts are negative or boolean is refused. The plan's identity is a digest of the confusion and the width and nothing else, and the corrected distribution is a classical vector rather than a quantum state, so nothing here restores positivity, enforces a rank, or reports an entanglement property.

### Trotterized time evolution of a Pauli sum

Turn a weighted Pauli sum into the circuit a product formula applies, so exp(-i * t * H) becomes an ordinary Circuit, with the exact circuit for one Pauli word's exponential underneath it.

- **Maturity:** Development evidence
- **Public API:** `flagquantum.algorithms.trotter`, `flagquantum.algorithms.pauli_exponential_circuit`, `flagquantum.algorithms.trotter_circuit`, `flagquantum.algorithms.TROTTER_ORDERS`
- **Runtime modes:** `local_statevector`
- **Hardware:** `cpu`
- **Gradient support:** `exact`
- **Distribution semantics:** `single_device_fast_path`
- **Start:** [quick example](../../examples/algorithms/trotter.py)
- **Documentation:** [guide](../../docs/guides/ALGORITHMS.md)
- **Known boundary:** A product formula and the exact circuit for one Pauli word, and nothing about accuracy. The circuit approximates exp(-i * t * H) and the approximation is exact only when the Hamiltonian's terms commute; otherwise the defect falls off with the step length at the composition's own rate, measured as a ratio of 2.007 at order 1 and 4.027 at order 2 per doubling of the step count on a three-wire transverse-field Ising Hamiltonian at t = 0.4, coupling 0.7 and field 0.5. No bound on that defect is computed or reported, because a bound needs a commutator norm the caller has to supply, and nothing here knows the caller's Hamiltonian; the module says so where the number would be. The exact side of every comparison the tests make is torch.matrix_exp of the dense generator, which exists at the sizes this repository simulates, so no rate here is a sampling result and no scaling claim follows. order is limited to 1 and 2: the higher-order Yoshida and Suzuki compositions spend more exponentials than they buy until a caller measures otherwise, and the module records that rather than offering them. order=1 is the Lie-Trotter product and order=2 the symmetric one, so a caller who asks for order 2 pays twice the exponentials and gets one order more of cancellation per step. Four refusals are deliberate. A term that is a multiple of the identity exponentiates to a global phase, and no gate in this repository applies one -- Circuit and CircuitIR carry no global_phase field -- so such a term is refused by the index that names it rather than dropped, because dropping it leaves a circuit right about the observable and wrong about the state; a coefficient with an imaginary part is refused because it names a non-unitary step; a word addressing a wire outside the declared register is refused rather than the register widened; and an order, step count, time or coefficient outside the accepted domain is refused before any circuit is built, so a rejected call leaves nothing half-made. A word whose support is only identity characters is refused for having no support, and the register-width check precedes it, so a zero-width register reports the width rather than the word. The word reader underneath the primitive is flagquantum.simulation.pauli's own, promoted from private for this unit, and it owns the character set, the length match against the target list, the target-uniqueness and non-negativity checks; this unit adds no second copy of any of them. The declared term order is preserved and never sorted, because a product formula's value depends on the declaration order and sorting would be a second, silent parameterization. There is no exp_pauli opcode: CUDA-Q applies exp(-i * theta * P) as one opaque instruction and decomposes it in its compiler, while this unit emits the decomposition -- a basis change, a CX ladder anchored on the word's first supported wire, and one rz at twice the angle -- which is what lets the static resource estimator count the gates, a router move them, and autograd reach a tensor coefficient through them. Measured on one term with a tensor coefficient at 0.6 and order 2: the readout is 0.715813373694 and its derivative is -0.161660517061. The ladder emits its inverse CX run in the same wire order rather than the reverse, which is valid only because every CX shares one target and its controls are distinct. This is a circuit constructor, not an execution path: it produces a Circuit and the existing runtime executes it, so no new target, runtime option, result field or execution mode is added, and no Stable Core export follows. Not here: error bounds, time-dependent Hamiltonians, qubitization as a public walk object, QSVT, and double factorization. The block encoding this unit sits beside has since been promoted out of svd.py into flagquantum/algorithms/primitives/block_encoding.py, which carries its own capability entry.

### Variational solvers over a checked cost operator

Fit QAOA angles to a weighted MaxCut graph through one checked edge list that reaches both the cost layer and the operator the energy is scored against, build the MaxCut cost operator itself, and expose that same objective as a plain callable of the angle vector so a gradient-free optimizer can be handed it, with the uniform superposition's stationary point and the finite-layer approximation carried on the result.

- **Maturity:** Development evidence
- **Public API:** `flagquantum.algorithms.variational`, `flagquantum.algorithms.maxcut_hamiltonian`, `flagquantum.algorithms.run_qaoa`, `flagquantum.algorithms.maxcut_objective`, `flagquantum.algorithms.QAOAResult`, `flagquantum.algorithms.QAOA_ASSUMPTIONS`, `flagquantum.algorithms.QAOA_LIMITATIONS`
- **Runtime modes:** `local_statevector`
- **Hardware:** `cpu`
- **Gradient support:** `exact`
- **Distribution semantics:** `single_device_fast_path`
- **Start:** [quick example](../../examples/algorithms/variational_solvers.py)
- **Documentation:** [guide](../../docs/guides/ALGORITHMS.md)
- **Known boundary:** A solver family over one ansatz, and not a new simulator, a new cost model, or a sampled execution path. What run_qaoa adds to the primitives beside it is agreement: a QAOA circuit is built from a list of weighted ZZ edges while an energy is an expectation against some Hamiltonian, and nothing inside either one relates the two, so a caller can build the ansatz for one graph and score it against another and every number that comes back is then a number about a problem nobody posed. The edge list is checked once and reaches the circuit and the operator through the same tuple, which removes that freedom for this entry point and only for it: a caller who assembles qaoa_circuit and qaoa_loss themselves still has it, and nothing warns them. The operator is sum over edges of w_ij Z_i Z_j with no constant term, so its value on a computational basis state is the edge total less twice the cut and its minimum is W - 2 * (largest weighted cut); the QUBO route through max_cut_qubo and qubo_to_ising describes the same minimizers but is an affine image of it -- the constant changes no angle while the halving of every coefficient does -- so the operator here is the one whose coefficients the cost layer rotates by, and reusing the QUBO form for an angle sweep would search a rescaled landscape. A negative weight inverts that one edge's preference and is allowed; a self-loop is refused because Z on one qubit squares to the identity and the edge is then a constant shift rather than a cut; and a repeated undirected pair is refused rather than summed, because summing would count the edge twice in the objective while the cost layer rotated twice as well and the two errors would reinforce. The premise the method cannot check is where the start is: the first layer's cost and mixer both act on the uniform superposition, which is an eigenstate of every ZZ and every X, so the measured gradient at a zero start is exactly 0.0 for every parameter at every layer count -- measured on a triangle and a five-edge four-node graph, one gradient entry per parameter -- and a zero start therefore returns the zero start with the objective unchanged. The start is consequently a required argument and this unit does not perturb it, because a default displacement chosen here would be a claim about the caller's instance rather than a property of QAOA. A finite layer count is an approximation and not an exact solver: on the five-edge four-node graph at a constant start of 0.1, one layer reaches -1.4735270738601685, two layers -1.914364218711853 and three layers -2.9724555015563965 against an optimum of -3.0, so a larger layer count is not guaranteed to be closer in general even though it is here, and the energy is an expectation rather than a bound. The trajectory the result carries is a record and not a descent: Adam, the default, takes a step of the learning rate's size regardless of how flat the objective is, so history can rise, and only the final value is the result. Angles, the circuit and the objective are float32 because the circuit builder casts every angle to float32, so a float64 start is downcast rather than refused and the reported energy is float32-accurate. The update inside run_qaoa is autograd-based and its optimizer_factory is a torch.optim optimizer, so the gradient-free units this package ships -- SPSAOptimizer and NelderMeadOptimizer -- cannot be supplied in that slot: an SPSA step is a rate-limited update from two objective values rather than a torch.optim optimizer, and a simplex is a whole run released at once rather than one either, so one parameter could not carry both shapes without a dispatch hiding which method ran. The route is the objective instead. maxcut_objective returns the very callable run_qaoa minimizes, checked through the same edge list and split the same way, and it is readable with float() without a backward pass, so either gradient-free unit can be handed it; measured on the one-layer triangle from a flat 0.05 start, Adam reaches -0.9999997615814209 in 300 steps, SPSA -0.9999911189079285 in 400 steps at 1201 evaluations and Nelder-Mead -1.0000003576278687 in 706 evaluations at maxiter 200. Nelder-Mead reaches the optimum while reporting converged=False, because that flag measures the simplex collapsing and not the energy arriving, and SPSA reports no flag at all; what none of the three does is sample, so no route here carries shot noise and the route is not a shot-based or hardware execution path. The result carries the fitted angles and the energy at them and nothing about the cut: sampling the optimized circuit, decoding a bitstring, ranking sampled cuts and comparing a sampled best against the optimum are all the caller's steps, and no cut value is returned. Not here: a cost Hamiltonian for a general QUBO or Ising instance beyond the QUBO conversions flagquantum.algorithms.qubo already carries, any constraint, penalty or slack term, a warm start from a classical heuristic, a multi-start or basin-hopping wrapper, and a shots or hardware path. The VQE entry points are deliberately not part of this entry: they have their own registered capability, algorithms_vqe_solvers, because their premise and their reading of converged are not this unit's. Demonstration scale: weighted MaxCut instances of three to five edges on three to four qubits, fitted on the CPU statevector path for one hundred steps. No performance, scaling, hardware or fault-tolerance claim follows from it.

### Variational eigensolvers over a caller-supplied ansatz

Minimize a Pauli sum over a circuit builder the caller supplies, by descending a fixed ansatz, by growing one operator at a time from an operator pool through an exact screened gradient, by running one objective under a schedule of optimizer stages, or by deepening a depth-indexed ansatz while keeping the coordinates the shallower stage already optimized, with the reference state's stationarity and the meaning of the converged flag carried on the result. The same problem can also be held as one value rather than re-assembled at each call: an Ansatz binds a builder to the parameter count it accepts, a VQESolver binds the operator, the ansatz and the optimizer factory, and vqe_objective hands that problem's energy to the constrained and simplex optimizers the autograd entry points cannot stand in for.

- **Maturity:** Development evidence
- **Public API:** `flagquantum.algorithms.Ansatz`, `flagquantum.algorithms.VQESolver`, `flagquantum.algorithms.vqe_objective`, `flagquantum.algorithms.run_vqe`, `flagquantum.algorithms.run_adapt_vqe`, `flagquantum.algorithms.run_hybrid_vqe`, `flagquantum.algorithms.run_layerwise_vqe`, `flagquantum.algorithms.VQEResult`, `flagquantum.algorithms.AdaptVQEResult`, `flagquantum.algorithms.AdaptVQEIteration`, `flagquantum.algorithms.LayerwiseVQEResult`, `flagquantum.algorithms.OptimizerFactory`, `flagquantum.algorithms.vqe_loss`, `flagquantum.algorithms.optimization.OptimizationStage`
- **Runtime modes:** `local_statevector`
- **Hardware:** `cpu`
- **Gradient support:** `exact`
- **Distribution semantics:** `single_device_fast_path`
- **Start:** [quick example](../../examples/algorithms/vqe_solvers.py)
- **Documentation:** [guide](../../flagquantum/algorithms/README.md)
- **Known boundary:** A solver family over one caller-supplied ansatz, and not a simulator, a cost model, a sampled execution path, or an accuracy claim. Every energy here is an exact statevector expectation: nothing samples, so no route carries shot noise and none is a hardware path. The premise this family cannot check is the reference state the operator pool acts on, and it is the premise ADAPT-VQE's screen rests on. Screening appends a candidate at angle zero and differentiates the objective at that angle, which is exact rather than estimated, so a reference state that is an eigenstate of every pool generator screens every direction to exactly zero. Measured on two qubits against the transverse-field operator ZZ - X0 - X1, whose ground energy is -2.2360679774997898: the pool (ry, rz, rx) on wire 0 from the |00> state selects pool index 0 at a screened gradient of -1.0 and descends to -1.4142135623730354, while the same pool from the |++> state selects nothing, takes no adapt iteration, reports converged=True and returns -2.0. The loop cannot tell a direction that does not help at this reference state from one that would help at another, and nothing reports the distinction. converged reports why the loop stopped and not how close the energy came, which is the same reading NelderMeadOptimizer's flag is documented with. Measured on the same instance: a pool of one with a zero tolerance reports True at -1.4142135623730354, a tolerance of 1e-1 reports True at -1.4142135623730951, and a run limited to one adapt iteration of one optimization step reports False at 0.999999999, so the run reporting the exit is the one closest to the ground energy and the flag is an exit test in neither direction. The objective is the caller's circuit builder, and nothing checks that the builder applies every operator the pool selected or that it reads every coordinate it was handed. What is checked is the one case torch reports unhelpfully. A descent needs an objective that depends on what it updates, and torch raises RuntimeError: element 0 of tensors does not require grad and does not have a grad_fn, which names neither the run nor the cause, so both entry points check first and name both: run_vqe with a builder that ignores its parameters raises ValueError beginning run_vqe: the objective carries no gradient, and run_adapt_vqe with a pool element that adds no dependence at the appended angle raises the same through run_adapt_vqe. A builder that reads only some of its coordinates is not caught, because the objective does carry a gradient then: an adapt run whose builder applies only its first selected operator selects and optimizes normally. run_vqe's returned energy is a fresh evaluation of the returned parameters and not the last history entry, and the two are different measurements: history records the objective at the start of each step, so on this instance a fifty-step run reports -2.2241294384002686 while the smallest value history held is about -2.2308, because the final Adam step moved the objective up. The result is where the run stopped, not the best point it visited, and no entry point keeps a best-so-far. Angles and energies are float32: run_vqe casts a float64 start down rather than refusing it, and a six-hundred-step run on this instance reaches -2.2360680103302002, which is 3.3e-08 below the operator's own double-precision ground energy, so a reported energy is float32-accurate and not a bound. run_layerwise_vqe is a sequence of fresh descents rather than one warm-started descent: each depth pads the new coordinates with zeros and re-runs the same objective from the previous stage's parameters, so a deeper stage starts where the last one finished rather than from an unrelated point, and the result carries one record per depth rather than one trajectory. A deeper stage can therefore report a higher energy than a shallower one on a general instance, and this unit makes no monotonicity claim. run_hybrid_vqe's stages are the optimization module's, so the methods available are that module's gradient-based ones and no gradient-free optimizer can be handed a stage even though the package ships two. A solver object is present in flagquantum.algorithms.solvers and it is a surface over these loops rather than a second stack: VQESolver holds the operator, the ansatz and the optimizer factory as configuration, Ansatz holds a builder with the parameter count it accepts, and vqe_objective hands the same energy to the constrained and simplex optimizers that cannot be supplied as a torch.optim factory. The count is a field rather than a question asked of the builder, because a builder is a function of one angle vector and cannot say how many angles it needs without being called, and there is deliberately no name-to-factory table, so an ansatz this package does not ship is configuration by constructing Ansatz directly. VQESolver.run is run_vqe and VQESolver.objective is vqe_loss, asserted bitwise rather than approximately, so the four loops above and the three gradient-free optimizers remain the only loops.
