Hamiltonian.from_observable converts a mapped molecule's Observable into the form these loops evaluate, carrying every stored term including the mapping's identity shift, which lands as a term with no factors rather than as a separate offset field; it merges nothing, so the stored count is the observable's count and the residue a symmetry cancels at about 1e-17 stays in it, which is why the counts quoted anywhere here are taken over the terms that carry a coefficient and not over the terms that are stored, and VQESolver.for_molecule takes the electron count and the operator from the one molecule object that holds both. Measured on H2 at 0.7414 Angstrom in four spin orbitals: the UCCSD reference energy at zero angles is the molecule's Hartree-Fock energy to 5.8e-15 in complex128, a two-hundred-step descent from zero reaches -1.1372701746530618 against an FCI energy of -1.137270174660663, NelderMeadOptimizer reaches -1.1372701746606584 over 689 evaluations of the hand-off, and CobylaOptimizer capped at 0.1 on the doubly-excited angle returns feasible=True with a largest violation of 0.0 at -1.130846513668226, so the constraint is reported satisfied and its price is 0.0064 relative to the exact ground state. Not here: an explicit penalty, slack or equality-constraint term, because a constraint is a feasible region handed to the constrained optimizer rather than a term in the operator; an adaptive QAOA that grows a mixer or a cost layer from a pool; a warm start taken from a classical heuristic inside an entry point rather than supplied by the caller; a sampled path; and any shots, hardware, accelerator or distributed route. Demonstration scale: two-qubit model operators with circuits of two to eight parameters, and one four-spin-orbital H2 whose three-angle UCCSD ansatz is fitted on the CPU statevector path, for one hundred to six hundred steps. No performance, scaling, hardware or fault-tolerance claim follows from it, and the chemistry section fixes one geometry, one basis set and the default active space rather than selecting among them.

### Stabilizer code stated as parity-check matrices

State a Calderbank-Shor-Steane code as its Z-type checks, its X-type checks and its logical operators, and get back the code record the declared families produce, with a distance computed from the matrices rather than read off the logical operator that was written down; the triangular colour patch, the square-lattice torus, the bivariate-bicycle family and the punctured Reed-Muller family arrive this way, with their matrices -- and, for the last of them, their logical operators too -- derived from a rule rather than written by the caller, or state a pair of parity-check matrices on its own and have the logical operators derived from them.

- **Maturity:** Development evidence
- **Public API:** `flagquantum.qec.CssCode`, `flagquantum.qec.css_code_matrices`, `flagquantum.qec.CssCodeMatrices`, `flagquantum.qec.triangular_colour_code`, `flagquantum.qec.toric_code`, `flagquantum.qec.bivariate_bicycle_code`, `flagquantum.qec.qldpc_code`, `flagquantum.qec.reed_muller_code`
- **Runtime modes:** `local_statevector_dynamic_hybrid`
- **Hardware:** `cpu`
- **Gradient support:** `unsupported`
- **Distribution semantics:** `single_process`
- **Start:** [quick example](../../examples/qec/css_code_from_matrices.py)
- **Documentation:** [guide](../../flagquantum/qec/README.md)
- **Known boundary:** A declaration route, not a code library. It admits a code whose checks and logical operators can be written as 0-or-1 rows over the data wires, which is the CSS class and nothing outside it: a mixed X-and-Z stabilizer has no row, because stating one would mean writing it as a row of each family and the pair of matrices would then describe checks that do not commute. That is a limit of the matrix route and not of the check protocol, which measures a mixed stabilizer with one ancilla and both CNOT directions; a code whose checks are all mixed is declared as a record instead, and ZxxzSurfaceCode is the first one. The matrices are Python sequences of sequences, because this module holds no array dependency and states none; a tensor block reaches the route only through the `.tolist()` the refusal names. Every invariant is checked rather than assumed: the two check families commute, each declared logical operator commutes with the opposite check family while not lying in the span of its own, the declared logical operators of a family pair up non-degenerately, the blocks agree on a width that leaves at least one logical qubit, no row acts on no data qubit, and each family declares as many operators as the width and the two ranks leave logical qubits. The distance is searched for rather than read off the representative the caller wrote down, which is what makes it a property of the code: the same Steane record reports three whether its logical operator is written at weight three or at weight seven. That search is bounded, and the bound is the caller's to accept: it tries wire subsets in increasing weight up to `distance_search_weight`, which defaults to three, and refuses when the bound is exhausted instead of reporting the best it found. The cost of the bound grows with the data-qubit count, and the route is demonstrated from eight to seventy-two data qubits rather than claimed as a distance algorithm for a large code; the seventy-two-qubit case is the published instance of the bivariate-bicycle family and its search is the one that takes measurable time, about twenty seconds against no measurable time for the eighteen-qubit member of the same family. A code whose two distances differ is where the bound bites hardest, and reed_muller_code is the first record here whose two family distances are not equal: the punctured Reed-Muller convention it states has an X distance of seven beside a Z distance of three, so the default bound of three refuses the code, the caller must ask for a bound the search reaches, and the record then reports the smaller number as the distance and both numbers separately. Which block states which check family is a convention rather than a fact about the code, and the rule states the one the parity contract names; exchanging the two blocks is the same code with the two Pauli families renamed, which was measured before the rule was written rather than assumed. The bound is also why that route stops at width five: the least X-type operator has weight seven, so proving it means exhausting every subset of weight up to six, which is affordable over the fifteen and thirty-one points of widths four and five and not over the sixty-three and one hundred and twenty-seven of widths six and seven, where the same search would look through 75611760 and 5434287328 subsets. The rule is stated for every width at or above four and the route refuses above five by name, so the narrower reach is the route's cost rather than a claim about the family. The refusal is per family and fail-closed rather than a fallback to the lightest operator it found, which is what makes the bound a statement about the search rather than about the code. Nothing here decodes, protects a logical qubit, or states a threshold, a logical-suppression figure or a family's error rate. Two routes reach a record and they are distinguished by where the matrices come from: a caller writes them, as the torus the golden path walks through does by hand, or the route derives them, as triangular_colour_code and toric_code do for families this package declares. The second is what keeps a declaration from being copied from another framework's source, and it now covers four families: one patch, one periodic lattice, one qLDPC family, and one written on the Boolean cube rather than on a lattice or a group. A caller's own matrices reach that same derivation directly, so a code that arrives as a specification rather than as a family rule no longer has to have its logical operators written out with it. The last of those is the one whose rule names no logical operator at all, so the route derives those too, as a null space taken modulo the stabilizer span; the null space itself lives in flagquantum/qec/gf2.py rather than beside the family, because it is the same reduction over the same rows that every other question here is asked with, and the two steps built on it -- the complement of the stabilizer span and the symplectic elimination that follows it -- live in flagquantum/qec/codes/qldpc.py, which is what the bicycle family and a caller's own matrix pair both call. Two properties of the derivation are stated rather than implied. The logical count is the matrices' rank and not their row count, so a construction that subtracted checks would report no logical qubit at all and refuse the smallest instance. And each family comes back in the order the symplectic pairing puts it, so a record's own lz[i] and lx[i] are partners rather than merely paired in some order. The derivation is held to an instance nobody here chose: over Z_6 * Z_6 with A = x^3 + y + y^2 and B = y^3 + x + x^2 the record reports [[72, 12, 6]], the parameters the literature states for that pair. tests/qec/test_bicycle_code.py keeps that instance behind a slow marker for its cost and measures the properties of the built record on the eighteen-qubit member instead, which is free. The golden path holds the two descriptions of the torus against each other on everything the edge numbering cannot change -- the qubit and check counts, the distance, the logical-operator count and the full mechanism-weight histogram of the detector error model -- because the caller's numbering and the record's are different and no attempt is made to relabel one into the other. The generic entry point has since landed as flagquantum.qec.qldpc_code, which takes an arbitrary parity-check matrix pair and derives its logical operators, so the route is no longer demonstrated only for the families this package declares. It was held to the four codes whose logical operators are declared elsewhere before it landed -- the Steane record, the torus at two sizes and the bicycle family -- and the parameters alone would not have held it, because a complement of the stabilizer span is not unique and a wrong representative of the right class reports the same count and the same distance. The test therefore requires every derived operator to differ from its declared partner by a check of its own type. One of the two properties above is only visible through this route: on the distance-three torus a derived Z-type operator spans seven data qubits while the record reports a distance of three, because the distance is searched over the code rather than read off the derivation. What remains absent is the Reichardt and Floquet families, which have neither a record nor a matrix route, and no family here states a gauge subsystem. Two refusals belong to the sibling routes rather than to this one and are inherited: a declared product that is not pure X or pure Z is refused by the memory circuit, and a model whose mechanisms flip three or more detectors is refused by the matching decoder, which is what a code whose lightest logical operator is not the lightest fault looks like from the decoder's side -- a refusal the colour code runs into deliberately, and which the belief-propagation decoder does not share.

### Exact density-matrix execution

Execute a circuit as a dense square density matrix so a channel acts on it exactly, then read probabilities, samples, counts and Pauli expectations off it through an exact autograd graph.

- **Maturity:** Development evidence
- **Public API:** `flagquantum.simulation.density_matrix.density_matrix`, `flagquantum.simulation.density_matrix.density_matrix_from_ir`, `flagquantum.simulation.density_matrix.apply_unitary_density`, `flagquantum.simulation.density_matrix.apply_kraus_density`, `flagquantum.simulation.density_matrix.expectation_z_density`, `flagquantum.simulation.density_matrix.expand_operator`
- **Runtime modes:** `density_matrix`
- **Hardware:** `cpu`
- **Gradient support:** `exact`
- **Distribution semantics:** `single_device_fast_path`
- **Start:** [quick example](../../examples/density_matrix_execution.py)
- **Documentation:** [guide](../../flagquantum/simulation/README.md)
- **Known boundary:** Exact evolution of a dense 2^n by 2^n square density matrix: the representation is exact and the cost is 4^n. Measured here up to 13 wires, where one complex128 density matrix is 8192 by 8192 and about 1.07 GB (4^13 times 16 bytes), and nothing above that is claimed. Readout has a separate and lower ceiling than storage, because probabilities over more than 8 wires are refused until max_marginal_wires is raised explicitly. Wire 0 is the highest-order index, so expand_operator of a one-wire gate on wire 0 of two wires is the gate tensored with the identity, not the identity tensored with the gate. mode='density_matrix' selects the route, and mode='auto' selects it exactly when the program carries a channel instruction; mode='statevector' and mode='mps' refuse such a program by name rather than approximating it. The engine is written in device-generic torch and reads the operands' own device and dtype, but accelerator execution is unverified because this checkout has no accelerator: device='meta' and device='mps' are refused at runtime backend admission, where the pytorch backend exposes cpu plus discovered accelerators, rather than by the engine, and the registry records cpu hardware only. The density matrix is not sharded: no rank and no second device partitions it. memory_limit_bytes is a capacity this route enforces rather than only records: a run whose dense state exceeds the declared limit is refused with CapabilityError, naming the bytes the plan states and the limit that was declared, and the boundary is inclusive, so a limit equal to that figure executes and one byte below it does not. The comparison is against the plan's own state_bytes, which is both the figure the plan publishes and the figure the run allocates: the plan's resolved batch size times the squared state dimension times the element size of the resolved precision, so the refusal needs no second cost model. Nothing smaller is offered as a substitute, because the representation is exact and dense, no rank and no second device partition it, and the batch axis is part of the allocation rather than something to iterate over one element at a time: the route does not silently drop the batch, narrow the precision, or approximate in order to fit. With no declared limit nothing is refused, since there is no capacity to hold the run to, and the advisory plan_runtime_selection cost selection is a separate planning-time mechanism that this route still does not consult. require_gradients is a planner admission and scoring input in the runtime, not a post-condition on the returned tensors, so a run that declares it with no differentiable parameter returns normally; the post-condition exists one layer up in flagquantum.lindblad, which raises gradients_not_available. The circuit IR does carry a mid-circuit measurement, a reset, and a classically conditioned instruction, and this route executes all three exactly: it keeps one dense density matrix per classical record, applies the outcome projector at each measurement, honours the recorded bit a condition reads, and sums the branches, so a measurement nobody reads is the dephasing channel it is rather than one sampled outcome. The route is reached through flagquantum.simulation.density_matrix.density_matrix on a flagquantum.dynamic.DynamicCircuit, which is what DynamicCircuit.density_matrix() calls. The sum is what a reader who ignores the classical register holds, so a caller who wants one branch's outcome still uses fq.experimental.dynamic.run_dynamic(..., shots=...), and fq.run and DynamicCircuit.state() still refuse a dynamic program by name rather than substituting one answer for the other. The number of branches is at most two to the number of measurements whose recorded bit some instruction conditions on; a measurement nobody reads costs one density matrix, not two, and a branch whose trace is exactly zero is dropped because it cannot occur. mode='auto' still selects this route on a channel instruction and not on a dynamic one, so a dynamic program reaches the engine through the circuit method rather than through the runtime mode. The default precision is complex64, where an exact gradient reproduces its analytic value to about 1e-8; precision='complex128' reproduces it bit for bit, measured at zero difference from -sin(theta) at theta=0.7.

### Dynamic-code record

State a code as the period of measurement phases that defines it, propagate the instantaneous stabilizer group through one period, and read back the period's protected count and least logical weight where the period is weakest.

- **Maturity:** Development evidence
- **Public API:** `flagquantum.qec.MeasurementPhase`, `flagquantum.qec.FloquetCode`, `flagquantum.qec.ring_floquet_code`
- **Runtime modes:** `not_applicable`
- **Hardware:** `cpu`
- **Gradient support:** `unsupported`
- **Distribution semantics:** `single_process`
- **Start:** [quick-start guide](../../flagquantum/qec/IMPLEMENTATION.md)
- **Documentation:** [guide](../../flagquantum/qec/IMPLEMENTATION.md)
- **Known boundary:** A record of a code, not a memory experiment and not a spacetime distance. The model here is one group per round and one operator per measurement on one time slice, so the number reported is the settled round's code distance and not a fault distance: no detector model is carried, a fault that is a measurement error in one round and a data error in the next is not one operator, and nothing here samples noise or decodes. What the record does derive, it derives from the schedule rather than from a table. The instantaneous stabilizer group a round leaves is the part of the previous round's group that commutes with every measured operator, and that subgroup is solved for -- one linear equation per measured operator written in the old group's coordinates, whose solutions are re-expanded into the old basis -- rather than read off the old basis one generator at a time, because a product of two generators can commute with a measurement although neither factor does; the suite exhibits the smallest witness of that difference rather than describing it. The settled period is the least number of rounds the group sequence repeats over, compared as spans and not as reduced bases, and the rounds before the first repeat are reported as the transient. Because a period is not one group, both reported numbers are read at every round of it: a round protecting a different count makes the period refused as not a code, and the least logical weight stated is the lightest of the rounds' readings, because a claim about a period has to hold where the period is weakest. The two readings are not the same kind of reading, and the difference is measured rather than described: a settled period whose rounds read different least logical weights is exhibited by the suite -- one of them weighs one and the other weighs two -- while no settled period whose rounds protect different counts has been found at all, and the count refusal is kept anyway, because a period that disagreed would otherwise report an arbitrary one of its rounds' counts. The search behind that sentence is recorded where the suite is: every two-round schedule on three wires was walked, every cycle of the advance map had a single rank, and so did the sampled longer schedules, the random schedules over widths three to seven, and every member of the ring family. The least logical weight is searched for in increasing weight over the two-times-width symplectic positions up to the caller's distance_search_weight, and exhausting that bound is a refusal rather than a number, so a record never reports the basis it happens to hold as a distance. Two families ship. The published ring is derived from its width alone and is small by measurement rather than by assertion: the two-phase ring on n wires settles at period two after one transient round, holds n / 2 + 1 checks and protects n / 2 - 1 qubits, and every one of the one thousand and fifty-three ring schedules of two, three and four phase classes over every width from four to twelve settles, protects at least one qubit and reads the same count at every round of its period, with no member reaching weight three and the three- and four-class rings reaching weight one. That is the family's honest ceiling: no ring schedule these sweeps reach has least logical weight above two, so the published ring is a record of the shape of a dynamic code rather than a distance claim, and the module says so beside the number rather than leaving the number bare. A schedule that settles protecting nothing is refused with the rank and the width named, which on rings of alternating families is what a period of four does, and a schedule that has not repeated inside max_rounds is refused rather than reported with a count read off a group that is still moving. Also absent: the baseline's dynamic-code surface carries gauge-measurement maps, record-defined logical maps and epoch schemas for authoring a period geometry, and none of those are here, because a schedule is stated as its rounds and no compiler or schema container is introduced for it. Nothing here is differentiable, distributed or accelerator-backed, and no threshold, logical-suppression or performance claim is made.

### Mixed-check code record

Read a patch whose every check carries an X factor and a Z factor on one ancilla as a stabilizer code, run its memory experiment through the shipped circuit and detector-model routes, and read what the mixed checks cost at the readout.

- **Maturity:** Development evidence
- **Public API:** `flagquantum.qec.ZxxzSurfaceCode`
- **Runtime modes:** `not_applicable`
- **Hardware:** `cpu`
- **Gradient support:** `unsupported`
- **Distribution semantics:** `single_process`
- **Start:** [quick example](../../examples/qec/mixed_check_code.py)
- **Documentation:** [guide](../../flagquantum/qec/IMPLEMENTATION.md)
- **Known boundary:** A record and its measured ceiling, not a decoder result. ZxxzSurfaceCode is the rotated surface patch after a Hadamard on the data checkerboard, so it occupies the same qubits, declares the same eight ancillas, carries the same eight checks and has the same distance as the rotated patch of the same distance; what changes is that every check comes out mixed -- an X-and-Z product on one ancilla -- and none is pure. Odd distances only, because the declared logical pair is the two diagonals and they miss each other at an even distance; the check is measured by one ancilla in both directions, so no gadget beyond the one flagquantum.qec.CodeCheck describes is needed, and the per-check measurement-flip vector the matrix route defines is refused here with the reason named. What the mixing costs is measured rather than asserted, and the number is the honest ceiling of this record: measured at distance three over three rounds with a one-percent data and measurement fault, four thousand shots and a fixed seed, the mixed patch and the rotated patch report raw logical rates of 0.0848 and 0.0838, which is one probability drawn twice, and the matching decoder then takes them to 0.0715 and 0.0092 -- a factor of 1.2 against 9.1. The reason is in the detector count rather than in the matcher: the rotated patch's model carries twenty-four detectors for eight checks over three rounds, sixteen of them the bulk one-per-check-per-round-boundary count and eight anchored to the preparation and the readout, while the mixed patch's carries exactly the sixteen bulk and no anchored detector at all, because a mixed check has no known value on a computational-basis preparation and is not recoverable from a Z-basis data readout. A fault that strikes the ends of the experiment is therefore outside the model, and no decoder can suppress a rate it cannot see. Closing that gap needs a readout plan that anchors a mixed check against a rotated-basis terminal measurement, which this package does not have; until then the record's suppression is bounded by its anchoring, which is what the example prints and what tests/qec/test_mixed_check_code.py falsifies. The record describes checks and observables, and chooses no decoding strategy, no noise model and no threshold. Nothing here is differentiable, distributed or accelerator-backed, and no threshold or logical-suppression claim is made.

### Bosonic operator algebra and its dense value on a truncated Fock space

Build and combine second-quantised bosonic operators with the canonical commutation relations applied at construction, and evaluate one as a dense matrix over a Fock space whose truncation dimension the caller names.

- **Maturity:** Development evidence
- **Public API:** `flagquantum.observables.boson`, `flagquantum.observables.boson.BosonOperator`, `flagquantum.observables.boson.BosonTerm`, `flagquantum.observables.boson.create`, `flagquantum.observables.boson.annihilate`, `flagquantum.observables.boson.number`, `flagquantum.observables.boson.position`, `flagquantum.observables.boson.momentum`, `flagquantum.observables.boson.identity`, `flagquantum.observables.boson.DEFAULT_DENSE_MATRIX_BYTES`
- **Runtime modes:** `not_applicable`
- **Hardware:** `cpu`
- **Gradient support:** `not_applicable`
- **Distribution semantics:** `not_applicable`
- **Start:** [quick-start guide](../../flagquantum/observables/README.md)
- **Documentation:** [guide](../../flagquantum/observables/README.md)
- **Known boundary:** A symbolic algebra and a dense finite-truncation reference. The existing static Lindblad input normalization consumes BosonOperator Hamiltonians, collapse operators, and low-level observable mappings with explicit dimensions by reusing to_matrix and its byte ceiling. examples/boson_interference.py demonstrates two-mode interference and independent loss; tests/unit/test_lindblad_boson_consumer.py checks analytic laws, cutoff, ordering, plan identities, batching, and initial-state gradients/adjoints. The algebra itself owns no execution entry point. There is no bosonic or photonic backend, no continuous-variable simulator, no Gaussian or phase-space representation, no squeezing, displacement, or beamsplitter primitive beyond what the ladder operators compose to, and no native Fock-space state, sampler, or trainable algebra coefficients. Static full-space Hamiltonian term mappings also accept boson operators paired with live scalar tensor coefficients; existing Parameter.bind resolves named values without changing the canonical algebra coefficients. examples/train_boson_coupling.py demonstrates an actual Adam update and tests/unit/test_lindblad_operator_coefficients.py checks two models, analytic gradients, complex conjugate pairs, batching, and plan equivalence. Serializing the bound numerical request does not serialize its autograd graph. The dense materialization remains a bounded reference rather than a scalable bosonic backend: it builds the Kronecker product over every mode and is refused above a byte ceiling of DEFAULT_DENSE_MATRIX_BYTES, which admits ten degrees at two levels each and refuses the eleventh. The truncation dimension is the caller's and is taken as a parameter rather than assumed, because the algebra has no finite matrix representation of its own: every creation operator raises the occupation without limit, so the commutation relations are applied here to untruncated monomials and the commutator of two truncated ladder matrices differs from the identity at the top level of every degree. Both statements are true of different operators, and only the explicit dimension says which one a caller's numbers describe. Two factors of the same kind and degree are a power rather than zero, so a monomial has no bounded length and the stored form is the Poincare-Birkhoff-Witt basis; equality is therefore structural rather than a tolerance, and two operators equal as matrices but not as canonical sums compare unequal. Exactly one transform to a qubit register ships, which is no transform: a BosonOperator is not an Observable and cannot be passed directly to fq.expectation; its materialized matrices can be used through the existing Simulation observable mapping. Nothing here executes, allocates a device buffer, or reads a runtime option.

### Fermionic operator algebra and its fermion-to-qubit images

Build and combine second-quantised fermionic operators with the canonical anticommutation relations applied at construction, and map a Hermitian operator to the Pauli Observable the existing execution path already measures, through either of two fermion-to-qubit encodings.

- **Maturity:** Development evidence
- **Public API:** `flagquantum.observables.fermion`, `flagquantum.observables.fermion.FermionOperator`, `flagquantum.observables.fermion.FermionTerm`, `flagquantum.observables.fermion.create`, `flagquantum.observables.fermion.annihilate`, `flagquantum.observables.fermion.number`, `flagquantum.observables.fermion.jordan_wigner`, `flagquantum.observables.fermion.parity_encoding`
- **Runtime modes:** `local_statevector`
- **Hardware:** `cpu`
- **Gradient support:** `not_applicable`
- **Distribution semantics:** `single_device_fast_path`
- **Start:** [quick-start guide](../../flagquantum/observables/README.md)
- **Documentation:** [guide](../../flagquantum/observables/README.md)
- **Known boundary:** A symbolic algebra and two transforms, and nothing that executes on its own. Two fermion-to-qubit transforms ship, Jordan-Wigner and the parity encoding, and they are the same operator written in two qubit bases: the parity image is the Jordan-Wigner image conjugated by the change of basis that replaces each mode's occupation with the parity of the modes up to it, which is measured rather than asserted, so a consumer written against Observable reads either one without being told which it holds. What the second basis buys is locality of the occupied-parity information and not a smaller image. Jordan-Wigner runs a Z string over the modes strictly below a factor's mode, so the first mode is a single Pauli and the last spans every wire; the parity encoding runs an X string over the modes strictly above it, so the last mode is one X plus one Z on the mode below and the first spans every wire. The widest factor image therefore costs one wire per mode in both encodings, reached at opposite ends of the mode list, and the reduction in worst-case Pauli weight that motivates the Bravyi-Kitaev and ternary-tree encodings follows from neither; those two remain absent. Both transforms take the mode count as an argument rather than inferring it, so a caller who names fewer modes than the operator addresses is the caller's error and is refused rather than silently widened, and both refuse a non-Hermitian operator for the same reason. Operators are stored normal-ordered against the vacuum, so equality is structural and a Hermiticity test is exact rather than a tolerance; the consequence is that two operators equal as matrices but not as canonical sums compare unequal, which is the documented boundary rather than a bug. The image of a structurally Hermitian operator has real dyadic coefficients under either transform and the imaginary part is checked against a fail-closed bound of 1e-9 rather than a tuned threshold, so a coefficient outside it is reported instead of having its imaginary part dropped, and no input this algebra builds needs the bound. There is no matrix form of a fermionic operator: the only numerical value is the Pauli image, which exists at the sizes the statevector path simulates. There is no circuit-level exponential of a fermionic operator, no molecular Hamiltonian assembly, no integral handling, and no sparse or dense second-quantised Hamiltonian; expectation values, sampling, and gradients follow from the image and the existing Observable path rather than from this module, which therefore makes no claim of its own about any of them. Nothing here executes, allocates a device buffer, or reads a runtime option, so the runtime mode named is the path its image feeds rather than a mode this unit runs.

### Belief-propagation decoder with ordered statistics over a detector error model

Decode a syndrome a minimum-weight matcher refuses, by iterating min-sum messages over the model's mechanisms and then solving the syndrome over the most error-prone independent ones, so a mechanism flipping three or more detectors is answered rather than declined.

- **Maturity:** Development evidence
- **Public API:** `flagquantum.qec.BeliefPropagationOsdDecoder`, `flagquantum.qec.BeliefPropagationOsdDecodeResult`
- **Runtime modes:** `not_applicable`
- **Hardware:** `cpu`
- **Gradient support:** `unsupported`
- **Distribution semantics:** `single_process`
- **Start:** [quick-start guide](../../flagquantum/qec/IMPLEMENTATION.md)
- **Documentation:** [guide](../../flagquantum/qec/IMPLEMENTATION.md)
- **Known boundary:** One decoder, in-process, over one detector error model at a time, with no batching, no streaming, no threshold, no logical-error-rate estimator, and no performance claim. The colour code was the first shipped family whose decoding evidence is this route rather than the matcher's, because its faces carry a single fault into three detectors and the matcher refuses the model: tests/qec/test_colour_memory_execution.py decodes every single fault of the executed distance-three patch and every syndrome the distance-five model samples, and asserts of each that the correction reproduces the syndrome it was given. The torus is the second, and it is the family where this route is measured as a rate and not as reproduction alone: tests/qec/test_toric_memory_execution.py takes the two-round declared model of the distance-three torus, whose eight weight-four mechanisms are exactly what moves it out of the matching decoder's reach, and asks every one of three hundred draws -- the empty syndrome included, which must be answered by selecting no mechanism -- for a set of mechanisms that reproduces it and for the observables they flip. At a one-percent fault rate the decoded logical rate is 0.0033 and 0.0100 against the model's exact raw rates of 0.1000 and 0.0933, and the margin that row asserts is a factor of four. The same measurement at the five-percent rate the file's other profile rows use is 0.2667 against 0.3588, a ratio of 1.34, and that number is recorded here rather than asserted into a claim: order-zero post-processing leaves the free mechanism positions at zero, so at that rate this decoder is a route to an answer and not a suppressed logical-error rate. The same file also records where the exact matcher's own budget bites -- at six rounds and two percent a sampled syndrome carries twenty-two detectors against its default twenty -- and that the windowed matcher refuses strictly fewer of those same draws while the hyperedge decoder and the PyMatching-backed name refuse none of them. It is a decoder and not an optimal one: belief propagation is an estimate, and the ordered-statistics pass holds every mechanism outside the elected information set at zero, so an answer reproduces the syndrome and need not be its most likely explanation. The exact claims are the measured ones. On the one-round Steane model the decoder reaches the least weight and the most likely observable for all 64 syndromes. On the one-round rotated surface code it carries more weight than the least-weight explanation on five of the 256 syndromes, by 0.6904 on two and 2.9444 on three, and differs from the most likely observable on three of those five, so the whole of its gap to an optimal decoder on that model is those five syndromes and not a property of the code. The iteration is min-sum with a caller-set damping factor, runs a caller-set number of passes, and reports whether it converged rather than looping until it does. The magnitude of a check message is capped, so a detector only one mechanism can flip forces that mechanism without producing an infinite message, and a mechanism whose stated probability is one or zero is floored before its log-odds ratio is taken so that the ratio stays finite. The decoder is reached through the name-keyed registry as get_decoder("belief_propagation_osd", model), and getting it named is why that registry states two protocols instead of one. Its DetectorErrorModelDecoder states what every registered name promises -- decode, returning the observables the correction flips -- and the pair graph moved to GraphlikeDetectorErrorModelDecoder, because a model carrying a three-detector mechanism has no pair-graph at all: DecodingGraph.from_detector_error_model refuses it as a hyperedge. The three matching names satisfy the graphlike protocol and this one does not, and a DecodingGraph handed to this name is refused with that reason rather than failing inside the constructor. The module imports torch and the standard library and nothing else, it is not wired to the stim sampling path, it reports the observables a mechanism set flips as a prediction rather than as a probability, and it is not calibrated.

### Subsystem-code record

Read a parity-check and gauge-operator table as a subsystem code: declare the checks, the gauge generators and the logical operators, and get back the number of protected qubits the checks and the gauge leave together, with the distance searched for under the dressed reading in which a logical operator is read modulo its own family's checks and gauge.

- **Maturity:** Development evidence
- **Public API:** `flagquantum.qec.SubsystemCode`, `flagquantum.qec.tesseract_code`, `flagquantum.qec.tesseract_column_swap`, `flagquantum.qec.tesseract_free_cnot_pairs`
- **Runtime modes:** `not_applicable`
- **Hardware:** `cpu`
- **Gradient support:** `unsupported`
- **Distribution semantics:** `single_process`
- **Start:** [quick-start guide](../../flagquantum/qec/IMPLEMENTATION.md)
- **Documentation:** [guide](../../flagquantum/qec/IMPLEMENTATION.md)
- **Known boundary:** A declaration record, not an experiment. SubsystemCode takes the same four blocks flagquantum.qec.CssCode takes and adds the two gauge blocks, and the two additions change what the four mean rather than adding to them: the tesseract's sixteen data qubits carry ten independent check rows and two anticommuting gauge pairs, so the checks alone leave six classes and the record reports four protected qubits beside two gauge qubits, which is exactly the family's four declared logical operators. The protected count is derived from the two check ranks and the gauge block's length rather than stated, so a caller who writes the count down does not get it honoured. Every invariant is checked: the two check families commute, every gauge generator commutes with every check, no gauge generator lies in its own family's check span, the two gauge blocks pair up non-degenerately, and each logical operator commutes with the opposite checks while lying outside its own family's checks *and* gauge. The distance is the dressed one and it is searched for rather than read off the declared operator, under the same bounded distance_search_weight the matrix route uses and with the same refusal when the bound is exhausted; the tesseract reports four under both readings, and the file measures the same four when the operator is additionally required to commute with the opposite gauge generators, so the published number is not an artifact of which center the search took. That the distance is dressed rather than bare is a measured claim and not a coincidence of the tesseract, whose two readings agree: the file carries a second family, the 3x3 Bacon-Shor, where reading up to the checks alone finds a weight-two operator and the record reports three. One thing is deliberately absent. This package has no round structure, no detector model in which a gauge generator is measured and no decoder registered for a subsystem protocol, so the record builds no memory circuit, samples no noise and reports no distance in the executable sense, and that absence is stated on its own measurement rather than on a citation. The record is also deliberately not a flagquantum.qec.StabilizerCode, so flagquantum.qec.css_code_matrices refuses it with the protocol named instead of building matrices the gauge generators would be dropped from -- a subsystem code is a second reading of matrices this package already accepts, not a second matrix class. One family ships, the tesseract, whose gauge tables are four representatives of the gauge span modulo the checks rather than the family's raw neighbouring pairs, because a gauge qubit is a pair. The search bound is the caller's to accept. The family's free-CNOT gadget is published beside the record rather than held by a test: tesseract_column_swap derives the sixteen-wire permutation from the hypercube layout -- a wire whose lowest coordinate is clear exchanges the column above it -- and tesseract_free_cnot_pairs states the two protected pairs the permutation is held to act on as CNOT(control -> target) and nothing else, so the claim that the permutation implements two free logical CNOTs joins two independently written facts instead of restating one. The derivation is not a claim about how the code looks: the tuple it returns was measured against the one the framework this package replaces publishes for the same family, wire for wire, on the same protected order. What the pair of functions does not do is compile the gadget, and the action they state is stated on the record's own protected order rather than against a port-named encoding. Nothing here is differentiable, distributed or accelerator-backed, and no threshold or logical-suppression claim is made.

### Huawei Ascend 910C native quantum workflows

Run native complex PyTorch simulation, gradients, hybrid training, noise, TEBD, native dynamics and QEC predecoder training on the fixed Ascend Torch-FL stack.

- **Maturity:** Development evidence
- **Public API:** `fq.Circuit`, `fq.Module`, `fq.run`, `fq.plan`, `fq.gradient`, `flagquantum.lindblad.run`
- **Runtime modes:** `statevector`, `mps`, `tensor_network`, `density_matrix`, `native_dynamics`, `qec_predecoder`
- **Hardware:** `huawei_ascend_910c`, `torch_fl_flagos`, `cann_9_0_0`
- **Gradient support:** `development_evidence_autograd_and_explicit_methods`
- **Distribution semantics:** `single_device_fast_path`
- **Start:** [quick-start guide](../../docs/guides/ASCEND_910C.md)
- **Documentation:** [guide](../../docs/guides/ASCEND_910C.md)
- **Known boundary:** Requires the exact source fixes and dependencies in the Ascend guide; published wheels alone are insufficient. The hardware record covers complex64/complex128 circuit workflows and the stated dynamics/QEC cases, with explicit CPU references, I/O and global QEC decoding boundaries. Optional JAX, CUDA-specific external simulators and quantum-cloud backends retain their own requirements. This is bounded hardware development evidence, not production, performance, capacity or hardware certification.


## Distributed execution

### Sharded statevector training

Partition one logical statevector workload across ranks while preserving differentiable training semantics.

- **Maturity:** Production supported
- **Public API:** `fq.plan`, `flagquantum.experimental.distributed.train_distributed_statevector`
- **Runtime modes:** `distributed_statevector`
- **Hardware:** `cpu`, `multi_gpu`
- **Gradient support:** `exact`
- **Distribution semantics:** `sharded_across_ranks`
- **Start:** [quick example](../../examples/distributed_statevector_topologies/run.sh)
- **Documentation:** [guide](../../examples/distributed_statevector_topologies/README.md)
- **Known boundary:** Target hardware is one host: the required two-GPU gate covers forward, backward, and training on two local devices with NCCL. A run spanning more than one host is the separate two-node capability, whose evidence does not extend back to arbitrary host counts.

### Two-node statevector training

Shard one logical statevector workload across two hosts and keep gradient, optimizer, and checkpoint semantics that a single host would produce.

- **Maturity:** Release certified
- **Public API:** `fq.plan`, `flagquantum.experimental.distributed.train_distributed_statevector`
- **Runtime modes:** `distributed_statevector`
- **Hardware:** `multi_node`, `nvidia_a800_sxm4_80gb`
- **Gradient support:** `exact`
- **Distribution semantics:** `sharded_across_ranks`
- **Start:** [quick-start guide](../../docs/guides/MULTINODE_RUNBOOK.md)
- **Documentation:** [guide](../../docs/development/TESTING.md)
- **Known boundary:** Certified scope is the recorded pair: two A800 hosts, up to four devices per host, and a matched statevector training workload of 22, 24 and 26 wires at depths 8, 12 and 16 with cross-shard gate fractions up to one half. The release gate evaluates the payloads promoted into benchmarks/results/scalability alongside the declared single-device baseline, and the checked-in result audit verifies their signatures and claim evidence; each promoted payload reports release_gate_allowed and scalability_claim_allowed true with an empty error list, and the matched pair leg reports a 5.73x speedup whose 0.95 confidence interval lies wholly above one. Inter-node shard exchange, the exact adjoint gradient, an owner-sharded optimizer step, a checkpoint resumed by a restarted run, and a full-state export all executed on that pair and are recorded by the hardware artifact. The whole amplitude vector is exported as the product of a leg of its own, reassembled by the runtime's own gather, so the readout is a materialization the workload needs rather than a gather taken to check an answer; that observation closed the validation-only full-state-gather blocker. The staging audit profiles the measured region and finds no explicit host transfer in it, which closed the host-staging blocker by measurement rather than by relabelling the region. What the certificate does not cover: the pair is the widest topology any measurement here reaches, and no third host was available, so two_node_pair_only_no_wider_topology stands as the pair's own scope and no measurement on this hardware can retract it; the hardware artifact's own latency leg is recorded at the narrowest rung of the workload this certificate freezes -- twenty-two wires over eight layers, one hundred and seventy-six bound parameters, read from the manifest the artifact names -- so the toy-circuit blocker that once applied to that leg is retired by that observation rather than by withdrawing the word; and GPU clocks, power state, temperature, host memory and fabric congestion are unrecorded, so the numbers describe this pair at the recorded revision and nothing wider. The pair's release world sizes are two, four and eight, all single-host except the matched pair leg, so behaviour as further hosts are added is not established.

A run spanning more than one host is the separate two-node capability, whose evidence does not extend back to arbitrary host counts. The only public capacity measurement is emitted from the validated claim below; it is one exact-workload result, not general scalability or release evidence. Boundary instructions still execute serially by owner, and layer-parallel contraction/SVD, capacity multi-step soak, a sealed fault matrix, repeated evidence, and the release payload remain incomplete.

### Two-node MPS training

Shard one logical matrix product state by site across two hosts and keep gradient, optimizer, and checkpoint semantics that a single host would produce.

- **Maturity:** Release certified
- **Public API:** `fq.plan`, `flagquantum.experimental.distributed.train_distributed_mps`
- **Runtime modes:** `distributed_mps`
- **Hardware:** `multi_node`, `nvidia_a800_sxm4_80gb`
- **Gradient support:** `exact`
- **Distribution semantics:** `sharded_across_ranks`
- **Start:** [quick-start guide](../../docs/guides/MULTINODE_RUNBOOK.md)
- **Documentation:** [guide](../../docs/development/TESTING.md)
- **Known boundary:** Certified scope is the frozen release topology: two A800 hosts of eight devices each, sixteen ranks over the inter-node RoCE fabric with the NCCL backend. The release gate evaluates the payloads promoted into benchmarks/results/scalability alongside the declared single-device baseline, the checked-in result audit verifies their signatures and claim evidence, and each promoted payload reports release_gate_allowed and scalability_claim_allowed true with an empty error list. The capacity leg runs the frozen 131072-site bond-768 workload for one sharded training step across those sixteen ranks, and it fails closed unless the backward-execution contract it asserts actually executed: the boundary adjoint exchange, the boundary gradient routes, the owner-sharded parameter, gradient and optimizer-update ownership, the per-rank backward memory plan and the per-rank backward communication plan are all reported production-measured or production-executed for that step, and the memory plan carries the per-rank byte vectors the readiness gate reads rather than a peak alone. The speed leg times the frozen matched-speed ladder at one device and at sixteen over the same pair, and the promoted payload reports a speedup of 1.1231339460338892 whose 0.95 confidence interval [1.099641001349927, 1.15942039054475] lies wholly above one. That ratio is measured against the workload's own shardable fraction and not against the world size: the frozen ladder's measured serial fraction is 0.8146523972125517, so sixteen ranks can at best return 1.2103070390553239 times one device, and the measured 1.1231339460338892 is 0.9279743980589623 of that ceiling. The efficiency against the world size is 0.07019587162711807 and is disclosed rather than claimed: the workload is dominated by work no rank can share, so what is certified is that sharding a workload this serial still gains about twelve per cent with a significant interval, not that the ratio approaches the rank count. The capacity premise is established by two signed measured_production_run envelopes at this contract's own digests: the same frozen workload exhausting one device with a recorded CUDA out-of-memory reason of 20.00 MiB refused against a 79.25 GiB device at a 75657923584-byte peak, and the same workload completing on sixteen ranks at a 77745422336-byte peak. What the certificate does not cover: the pair is the widest topology any measurement here reaches and no third host was available, so two_node_pair_only_no_wider_topology stands as the pair's own scope, and because the frozen release world of sixteen devices is exactly this pair's width, behaviour as further hosts are added is not established. The premise's two halves are two signed runs rather than one, and the single-device baseline's single_gpu_expected_oom is the release contract's expectation rather than a second measurement of the same run. The profiled measurement region of the layer-halo path still issues explicit host transfers that keep a control-plane readback blocker of their own -- fourteen load-balancing cost probes, five point-to-point control descriptors read back to size a receive buffer, four truncation record exchanges and five end-of-forward reporting reads, with no rank payload staged among them -- and this certification does not retire that blocker, because the release legs measure the training step rather than profile that region. The layer halo crosses the host boundary only on the compiled site-kernel path, which the checkpointed training legs deliberately do not take. GPU clocks, power state, temperature, host CPU and memory, fabric congestion and the driver version are unrecorded, so the numbers describe this pair at the recorded revision and nothing wider. Historical provenance this certificate supersedes but does not delete: the earlier eight-rank attempt at the same ladder is retained under benchmarks/results/smoke/release_candidates/mps_matched_speed_4x2 as a measurement the release contract refused, it sets no claim flag, and its payload still carries the plan shapes the strict audit rejected -- an absent optimizer_update_semantics, a backward memory plan without the per-rank vectors, and a backward communication plan without the boundary edge record shape -- so it must be read as superseded provenance rather than as the certified evidence. The former production_supported scope is likewise retained as measurement rather than erased: on the recorded two-A800 pair with one device per host, one six-wire complex128 circuit and a bond limit that truncates nothing, site-boundary exchange, an exact reverse gradient with the layer halo prefetched over the inter-node transport, an owner-sharded optimizer step and a checkpoint resumed by a restarted run all executed, the route was observed on the RoCE fabric, the cut width was swept rather than declared across five placements at widths two and four, and the whole MPS was exported as the product of a leg of its own through the runtime's own gather. The four-devices-per-host forward latency of one six-wire circuit at the ladder's 8192-site acceptance rung is recorded in benchmarks/results/local/multinode_two_node_performance_20261001.json, whose own blockers and false claim flags say it is a latency of one circuit and not a scaling, capacity or release claim. A fixed-workload capacity series at artifacts/development/mps_capacity_series_20261023/ measures this same 131072-site, bond-768, 31-parameter program at 1/2/4/8/16 ranks: the first four deployments exhaust CUDA memory and sixteen ranks complete one sharded Adam step, with a maximum per-rank peak of 77745422336 bytes. The new v2 envelopes verify SHA-256 integrity without a signing key. Failed allocations remain non-release baselines; the series does not widen the certified topology, replace the historical baseline, or remeasure matched speed. The archived per-rank records, logs, allocation policy and bounded launcher diagnostics disclose the measurement conditions.

### Two-node tensor-network training

Slice one logical tensor-network contraction across two hosts and keep gradient, optimizer, and checkpoint semantics that a single host would produce.

- **Maturity:** Production supported
- **Public API:** `flagquantum.simulation.tensor_network.run_tensor_network`, `flagquantum.experimental.distributed.train_distributed_tensor_network`
- **Runtime modes:** `distributed_tensor_network`
- **Hardware:** `multi_node`, `nvidia_a800_sxm4_80gb`
- **Gradient support:** `exact`
- **Distribution semantics:** `sharded_across_ranks`
- **Start:** [quick-start guide](../../docs/guides/MULTINODE_RUNBOOK.md)
- **Documentation:** [guide](../../docs/development/TENSOR_NETWORK_DISTRIBUTED_CONTRACT.md)
- **Known boundary:** Scope is the recorded pair: two A800 hosts with one device per host, one fourteen-wire complex128 circuit bound to the frozen ladder's own narrowest matched-speed rung -- eighty-four parameters over six layers -- and a cut of exactly two labels declared by the workload. Slice-boundary reduction, the exact slice-gradient sum, an owner-sharded optimizer step, and a checkpoint generation resumed by a restarted run all executed on that pair, and the run reports scalability_claim_allowed and release_gate_allowed false with one blocker attached. The route was observed on the RoCE fabric and five synchronized amplitude samples were recorded after two warmups, so the fabric and a repeated measurement are evidence rather than assumptions. The cut width was swept across both labels of the declared cut, which retired the blockers that named an unswept cut and a fixed slice count. The cut is still declared rather than chosen by the automatic slicer, which selects by peak memory and would here pick a label carried only by state-copy nodes whose partial is zero on every rank but one; slicing such a label would report sharded execution while one rank did the arithmetic. Congestion and capacity were not measured, the profiled measurement region issues no explicit host transfer, the whole state the workload produces is exported as a leg of its own rather than gathered to check an answer, and topologies wider than one device per host run on the lane but are not recorded, so this evidence does not establish behaviour as the cut widens or as nodes are added. This capability does not reach release_certified, and the reason is measured rather than inferred. Its release contract freezes a matched-speed acceptance configuration -- 180 trainable angles over eighteen wires and ten layers at six sliced labels -- and that configuration was measured on one A800 and exhausts it: 66.9 GiB peak and a further 16 GiB refused in 50.7 s, while the two narrower rungs of the same frozen ladder complete at 0.79 GiB and 8.53 GiB. The pair does not rescue it, because the reverse-mode peak is not slice-owned: the pair fails the rung with the same 58.9 GiB resident and the same 16 GiB refused. No matched-speed baseline or pair leg can be sealed from the frozen ladder while its reverse tape is unbounded, which is what 'missing_statistically_significant_speedup_artifact' reported for as long as every leg was run that way. Bounding the tape changes the peak without changing the arithmetic: one baseline leg at world one and one sharded leg at world two, both under a 4294967296-byte reverse-tape bound with the shape, slicing, parameters, steps and optimizer untouched, complete all three frozen rungs and seal as benchmarks/results/smoke/release_candidates/tensor_network_matched_speed_pair/tensor_network_matched_speed_pair.json, where the acceptance rung's interval [1.9821866944895041, 1.98656634565854] is wholly above one and the other two rungs report intervals that exclude one as well. The sealed candidate carries no claim flag, so the gate reads it as production evidence rather than as a release payload: with the four claim keys added it reports 'capacity_premise_not_established' alone, and without them it reports that blocker beside the four a claim-free payload cannot satisfy. The claim was measured rather than asserted -- a probe envelope sealed with --release-payload from the same two legs at the same revision passes the strict audit with no errors -- so a reader can reproduce the comparing verdict from the committed candidate by adding the claim keys the candidate deliberately omits, and tests/benchmark_contract/test_tensor_network_release_gate.py reads the committed artifact to assert both verdicts: that the claim-free candidate reports the four blockers a claim would answer beside the premise, and that the same evidence carrying those keys reports the premise alone while the strict audit accepts it with no errors. Those tests also rebuild the artifact's unsigned envelope and require the committed content_sha256 to be reproduced, so the file's own digest is what makes the comparison an observation rather than a transcription. The bound is a measurement condition the frozen speed_workload does not name: the payload records it as checkpoint_budget_bytes and its provenance command records it, but the release gate does not read it, so the candidate is evidence about this pair under a recorded bound rather than a certification of an unbounded ladder. Freezing that bound now would describe a protocol chosen to fit the hardware it was measured on, so the manifest is left naming no budget and the condition is disclosed here instead. The contract's capacity premise is unestablished for the same structural reason -- no measured shape both exhausts one device and completes sharded at one slicing -- so 'capacity_premise_not_established' cannot be cleared either. Both findings are recorded in the manifest rather than corrected by re-freezing the ladder after the measurement, which would let the freeze describe a protocol chosen to fit the hardware it was measured on. The host-staging blocker was retired by measurement rather than by relabelling: the audited amplitude region resolves its contraction plan on every rank instead of receiving it through an object collective, and it builds its basis projectors by indexing a device-resident identity instead of lifting the target table, so the region the probe profiles now reports no explicit host transfer at all -- twenty-three host-to-device copies at the revision this note previously described, then three, then none, each step read from the probe's own profiler sweep. The validation-only-gather blocker is retracted by measurement rather than by declaration: the probe runs an export leg of its own that materializes the whole distributed state as the product the workload needs, it refuses a state it did not materialize, and it asserts the placement that leg ran under, so the sliced full state is no longer gathered only to check an answer. Two later measurements widen the capacity finding rather than narrow it. First, the frozen capacity workload does not contract at its own default slicing at the revision the manifest declares: the executor's default slice-label selection picks the lowest-numbered contracted labels, which for the frozen 4x6x2 grid are its deepest, and the resulting operands exceed the twenty-five-dimension ceiling shared by torch.einsum and PyTorch's copy machinery, so the producer's capacity-failure role raises 'tensor has too many (>25) dims' before it measures any allocation. Spreading the label argument across the network instead completes the same rung in 4099927552 bytes, so the rung the ladder records as completing at 51191433216 bytes was measured with an explicit label argument the manifest does not name, and the ladder is not reproducible from the freeze. Second, every rung in the ladder was measured with an unbounded rematerialization tape, so the rungs that exhaust a device record the budget they ran under rather than the shape: bounding the reverse tape at 4294967296 bytes and holding shape, slicing, parameters and steps fixed turns the two-sliced-label 4x6x2 rung from 80930380288 bytes out-of-memory into a completion at 17160570368 bytes, and turns both 4x6x3 out-of-memory rungs into completions at 30072620032 and 27926225920 bytes. The budget is not monotone -- the four-sliced-label 4x6x2 rung completes unbounded and raises once bounded -- so neither the label choice nor the tape budget can be left implicit by a manifest that claims to have frozen a capacity premise. Both findings are recorded in the manifest's freeze_note and premise_established_note and in the runbook rather than folded into measured_ladder, which stays a record of what was run.

## Deployment and extension

### Bounded native Qmod source and checked execution

Translate a bounded pinned native Qmod subset into the verified Compiler construction profile and the existing checked Program, preserving source diagnostics, initialization, numeric significance and named CPU results.

- **Maturity:** Development evidence
- **Public API:** `flagquantum.synth.synthesize`
- **Runtime modes:** `statevector`
- **Hardware:** `cpu`
- **Gradient support:** `not_applicable`
- **Distribution semantics:** `single_device_fast_path`
- **Start:** [quick example](../../examples/algorithms/synthesis_source_trial.py)
- **Documentation:** [guide](../../flagquantum/ecosystem/_qmod/README.md)
- **Known boundary:** Unreleased pinned-source subset only, with one main and fresh unsigned scalar outputs; no general standard library, arrays/views, Input/Const, free/drop or .fq support. Original corpus: 3/3 parsed, lowered, executed and full-state verified at unchanged 1/23/24-qubit sizes on exact CPU complex128. Both file and text inputs passed these three originals. Services reuses the same checked Program and named reader with a default 512 MiB planned-state buffer ceiling; this is not a process peak-memory guarantee. Synthetic probes do not increase original counts. Source-version context is declared, not authenticated; retained manifests separately check original bytes. No all-corpus, release or hardware claim.

### Circuit packaging and cloud deployment

Package trained circuits, export provider formats, and route them through deployment provider abstractions.

- **Maturity:** Development evidence
- **Public API:** `flagquantum.deployment.create_deployment_package`, `flagquantum.deployment.deploy_circuit`
- **Runtime modes:** `provider`
- **Hardware:** `provider_dependent`
- **Gradient support:** `not_applicable`
- **Distribution semantics:** `provider_dependent`
- **Start:** [quick example](../../examples/train_parameterized_circuit_then_deploy.py)

- **Known boundary:** Provider support and credential/runtime behavior vary; no provider is release-certified by this matrix.

### Evidence-qualified QPU digital twins

Predict calibration-conditioned measurement distributions, prospectively validate fixed and held-out regional suites, and align comparable evidence over time.

- **Maturity:** Development evidence
- **Public API:** `flagquantum.twin`
- **Runtime modes:** `offline_density_prediction`, `offline_evidence_assessment`, `explicit_provider_submission`
- **Hardware:** `cpu`, `quafu_development_evidence`
- **Gradient support:** `unsupported`
- **Distribution semantics:** `single_process`
- **Start:** [quick example](../../examples/remote/quafu_twin_region_holdout.py)
- **Documentation:** [guide](../../docs/guides/QPU_DIGITAL_TWIN.md)
- **Known boundary:** Frozen mapped models, validation series, calibration and validation histories, prospective candidate comparisons, topology- and depth-qualified evidence, connected structural cell composition, fail-closed regional model composition, repeated exact-circuit validation, fixed regional suite validation, predeclared reference/holdout comparison with simultaneous confidence, longitudinal fixed-suite regional histories, and caller-configured offline freshness assessment are available. Agreement is total-variation agreement for classical measurement distributions, not quantum-state fidelity. Region-model composition never infers cross-cell correlated noise or combines local bounds into region accuracy. Holdout evidence applies only to the prospectively declared circuits and does not establish arbitrary-circuit accuracy or training generalization. Regional suite histories compare only the same ordered fixed suite, mapping, and full topology. Freshness assessment applies caller-supplied thresholds and never chooses a universal trust window; validation age is calibration-snapshot age because v1 evidence has no authoritative hardware result-completion time. Evidence remains specific to declared circuits, operations, mappings, physical couplers, depth, calibration snapshots, and confidence bounds. Automatic calibration collection, scheduling, arbitrary-circuit generalization, region-wide statistical inference, framework-owned trust policy, model promotion, global publication, and release-certified provider support remain outside the framework capability.

### Interoperability adapter contract

Implement and certify optional external-framework conversion behind one immutable lazy registry and framework-neutral, loss-aware result contract.

Common conformance does not install dependencies, sandbox third-party Python, certify provider hardware or numerical equivalence, or permit external objects to enter runtime and accelerator layers.

### PennyLane QuantumScript interoperability

Translate supported immutable PennyLane QuantumScript programs to versioned FlagQuantum IR and back through an isolated, loss-aware control-plane adapter.

QNode, device execution, shots, measurements, trainable parameters, arbitrary wire labels without explicit lossy flattening, and idle wire extents are outside v1. PennyLane objects never enter FlagQuantum runtime, Torch-FL, CUDA, vendor accelerator, or QPU layers.

### PennyLane Lightning execution bridge

Execute a FlagQuantum circuit explicitly on local PennyLane lightning.qubit while preserving FlagQuantum-owned result and evidence contracts.

- **Runtime modes:** `external_local_statevector`, `external_local_shots`

- **Known boundary:** The flagquantum.ecosystem.pennylane bridge executes one fully bound, single-batch FlagQuantum circuit on local CPU lightning.qubit and returns an owned ExecutionResult. It supports exact complex64 and complex128 statevectors and computational-basis samples or counts with explicit qubit order and seed. Explicit device wires preserve idle FlagQuantum qubit extent. It does not support gradients, QNodes, noise models, dynamic circuits, automatic routing, GPU, alternative PennyLane devices, or fallback. Native fq.run remains unchanged.
